    `instance-get` target, so the marker check crashes instead of
    validating. Non-emission calls through the handle
    (`w.task.status()`) compile fine. The harness's working spelling:
    call the worker's emission method bare (`w.task.run(prompt)`) from a
    supervisor method that is itself declared `emission` — the boundary
    is still marked, one level up. Fix: teach `_is_emission_call` (and
    the G4 message) about `instance-get` targets — walk the handle's
    provision to its service and check `emission` there. Blocked-on:
    none; it is the one crash in the subagent path.
83. ✅ **Durable host resources need a documented extern-`acquire` spelling
    (harness milestone 3).** The durable session store (real file handle
    via `extern acquire fn log_open(path) -> Int undo log_close(1)`)
    works, but two ergonomics surfaced: (a) the `undo` slot on an
    `acquire` extern takes a *call expression* (`log_close(1)`), so the
    fd being closed must be threaded through the component's own
    `effect log_open(...) undo log_close(fd)` — the extern-level undo is
    effectively documentation; (b) multi-line extern bodies must be
    column-0 until item 78's dedent lands (the harness keeps the
    workaround). Both are cheap to close; (a) wants a doc example of the
    real acquire/undo discipline with a resource that has state (the
    harness's `durable_log.rvl` is that example).
84. ✅ **`Map.keys()`/`size()` on the host `Map.new()` stub compile but
    crash at runtime on py (harness milestone 6, finding #18).** The
    session index needed `store.keys()` on a host-acquired
    `let store = effect Map.new() undo store.drop()`. The checker routes
    `keys()` to the stdlib builtin (it type-checks clean), but the py
    emitter lowers it as `sorted(store)` where `store` is a `Map`
    *object* (backends/python/runtime.py `class Map`), so
    `AttributeError: 'Map' object has no attribute 'keys'` at runtime.
    Same class as the FR-4 rust bug, inverted: the *host* Map family
    surface (new/insert/remove/get/drop) never gained the iteration
    methods, but the builtin table makes the checker accept them anyway.
    Fix: either emit `sorted(store.data)` for host-`Map` receivers on py
    (and the tier analogues), or refuse `keys`/`size` on host-Map
    receivers at the checker with a "host Map has no iteration — use the
    value `Map` (`Map.empty()` + `set`/`lookup`) or track keys yourself"
    hint. The harness worked around it by keeping a `__ids__` list inside
    the same host Map (reverted by the same effect accumulator).
85. ✅ **Multi-line string literals for self-hosting (harness milestone 6,
    FR-19 candidate).** revl strings have no escape sequences (`"a\nb"` is
    literal backslash-n), so an agent authoring a real `.rvl` component
    from inside the harness (the lighthouse shape) cannot hand the
    compiler a multi-line source without building it by concatenation.
    The admission tests had to flatten proposed components to one line —
    wrong for the actual use case. Suggest a triple-quoted
    `"""..."""` form (or documented line-continuation idiom) whose
    contents may contain newlines verbatim; six-tier lowering is trivial
    (the lexer already reads raw text between delimiters).
89. ✅ **Service-signature params carrying v3 record types collapse to
    `unknown` on the ts tier (harness milestone 6/7, finding #19).**
    The v1 service-interface renderer (`_ts_type`) does not know record
    names, so a service method taking `List[Msg]` (a record) emits
    `complete(history: unknown[])` — the interface exists (`export
    interface Msg`), but the param type collapses, and `tsc` then rejects
    every call site passing a real `Msg[]` (`Type 'unknown[]' is not
    assignable to type 'Msg[]'`). This is the ts half of the JSON
    protocol's multi-tier story (item 81): the RealModel provider
    (which takes `List[Msg]`) could not typecheck on the DSH tier.
    **Implemented on `agent/fr86-ts-record-params`**: `_ts_type` falls
    through to `_ts_v3_type` for unknown names (records are emitted as
    interfaces), plus List/Opt/Map/Result generics; golden regenerated.
    Residual: the harness's `@ts` http body still returns a `Promise`
    (item 80 — async extern bodies), the last tsc error on the real
    provider.

100. ✅ **`_tool_admit` compiles before honoring `replacing` (harness model
    switcher, finding #23).** The harness's live model route swaps the
    `model` provider (mock -> real) by shipping the full composition with
    `replacing=["MockModel"]`. The admit stage in
    `src/revl/mcp/server.py::_tool_admit` first compiles the candidate
    WITHOUT `replacing` (line 660) and only re-compiles with it (line 668)
    if that first compile succeeded. A provider swap always fails the
    first compile with G2 ("key `model` is provided by both MockModel and
    RealModel"), so the replacing path never runs and every provider swap
    is refused. Fix: honor `replacing` on the FIRST compile (pass it into
    `_compile`), keeping the second compile only as a fallback for
    non-`files` candidates. Repro: the harness's `_switch_model` (ship the
    full composition with the other model provider). **Re-verified
    2026-08-24 against main 89c7685: still open.** The fused `revl_ship`
    path stops at the admit stage (`"stoppedAt": "admit"`, G2 diagnostics
    above); the standalone `revl_swap` tool honors `replacing` on its first
    compile (server.py line 297-300), so the fix is isolated to
    `_tool_admit`. Numbering note: main's item 94 (Async[T] erasure, ✅) is
    a DIFFERENT item — this text is authoritative for the admit-replacing
    fix.

101. ✅ **Rust emitter: reuse-after-move at a service-call argument (harness
    verification of item 93, finding #24).** Item 93 fixed three
    rust-emitter bugs and the mtier harness dropped from 7 cargo errors to
    1, but one remains: a provide method that passes a `String` by value
    into a service call and then returns it —
    `let _ = self.sessions.append(..., Msg { content: answer }); return
    answer;` — moves `answer` into `append` (E0382 use of moved value).
    The rust emitter needs `content: answer.clone()` when the argument
    variable is reused after the call (the same reuse analysis item 93
    applied to the loop's `current`, extended to service-call arguments).
    Repro: `revl run mtier/*.rvl --backend rust --once` in the harness
    repo. py and ts pass the same harness (2/2 lifecycle tests each) —
    this is rust-emitter-only, the last error between the string-protocol
    harness and the "runs on all runtimes" claim for the loop shape.

102. ✅ **Lifecycle tests cannot advance the clock, so a timer's *firing* is
    not provable in-language (harness verification of item 57, finding
    #25).** Item 57's contract has two halves: the schedule is revertible
    (unload cancels, residue-free) and the clock is a coeffect (a firing is
    a deterministic timeline step — "fires on the 3rd tick"). The harness
    proves the first half inside a `lifecycle test` (py + ts both PASS),
    but the second half is *not expressible in the language*: the lifecycle
    statement set is only `load`/`unload`/`call`/`assert`/`assert no_residue`
    (src/revl/parser.py `_LIFECYCLE_STMT_WORDS`, src/revl/lower.py
    `_lower_lifecycle_body`), and a firing happens only when the harness
    calls `Clock.advance(ms)` — which nothing in-language can do, and
    `asyncio.sleep` cannot produce (the clock never moves on its own). A
    revl author therefore cannot write "fires on the 3rd tick" as a
    lifecycle test; the harness proves it with a host driver
    (`tools/timer_demo.py` in revl-harness) that calls `Clock.advance`
    directly. Fix: add an `advance <ms>` lifecycle step — parser
    (`AdvanceStmt`), lowerer (`{"step": "advance", "ms": N}`), py emitter
    (`Clock.advance(N)`), ts emitter (`host.clockAdvance(N)`), and
    `revl test` doc — so the full item-57 contract is assertable in
    `revl test` on the reference tiers. Repro: `revl test
    src/timer_tests.rvl` in revl-harness proves cancellation but has no
    statement that could fire a tick.

103. ✅ **The harness's reader artifacts cannot cross the canonical ABI —
    `split`/`indexOf` are refused on the wasm tier (harness verification of
    item 41 slice-3, finding #26).** Slice-3 verified against the harness:
    `wasm/canonical.rvl` (the durable ledger's real pipe line format
    `log_line`, pure concat) emits, wasm-tools validates, and wasmtime's
    component model runs it (`revl:exported/ledger.log-line("s1","user",
    "hello")` → `"s1|user|hello"`, empty-string edge, exact round trips;
    milestone 31 in revl-harness). But the boundary carries only what concat
    builds. The harness's *reader* artifacts — the durable pipe **reader**
    and the agent's `add` tool (both `split`), and the web router's
    `route_request` (`indexOf`) — are refused by the wasm emitter
    (`unsupported builtin method 'split'`, `indexOf is not lowerable on this
    tier yet`), so the harness's full agent wire protocol cannot yet be a
    standard component. Fix: lower `split`/`join` and `indexOf` on the wasm
    tier (linear-memory forms exist in the reference backends; the wasm
    tier's restriction table already names them as hard EmitErrors). That
    closes the gap between "the Str boundary works" (slice-3) and "the
    harness's own toolbox/durable/web protocols are standard components".
    Repro: `tools/canonical_demo.py` in revl-harness (PASS) vs
    `add_tool` from `mtier/toolbox.rvl` (REFUSED: `split`).

## Toward early production

The items above complete and harden the *language*. These turn it into
something a second team can build on and operate. Multi-runtime stays (see IR
versioning and the Cordis backends); nothing here blesses a single tier.

### 1. Soundness: close or fence every gap, prove each with a rejection test

The one promise revl sells is that the checker is the admission gate: a
component that would corrupt a running system cannot compile. That promise is
only as strong as its weakest silent gap, so the soundness surface has to be
complete and self-proving.

- ✅ **Enumerate the contract.** Every guarantee G1-G8 and lifecycle rule
  A1-A8 is now accounted for in the executable spec. Ten (G1-G4, G6, G8, A1,
  A2, A6, A8) have a rejection fixture in `examples/rejections/` that the
  checker must refuse with the guarantee-naming diagnostic. The other six are
  **not independently checker-rejectable** — G5 and A5 are prevented by the
  grammar (an `effect` in an `undo`/teardown position is a parse error, and
  `compensate` is an optional slot with no "required-but-missing" program),
  G7 is derived-by-lowering (Thm. 16, not a compile check), and A3/A4/A7 are
  lowering transforms (identifiers renamed, `$$` escaped, flags advisory) —
  each documented, with where it is actually enforced, in a block after the
  `REJECTIONS` table. The one plausible hole checked (an emission reached from
  a teardown body) is sound: it stays on the G8 audit surface. So the suite
  *plus that block* is the definition of sound, not a sample.
- **Close the known holes** (today: "sound where declared, silent where
  unknown", item 12):
  - ✅ stdlib method return types are now typed on known receivers, so
    `charAt`/`charCodeAt` misuse is a compile error (`s.charAt(0) + 1` where
    an `Int` is expected is rejected). Host-*provenance* objects (an extern's
    return) still infer to unknown and stay silent — fenced in
    docs/contract-errata.md, on the G8 audit surface.
  - the "Typing follow-ups" under Next (generics instantiation, match-arm
    check-position, component-setup op sweep) are soundness work, not
    ergonomics; treat them as blockers here.
  - **stratum-3 function values are unchecked** (function-types.md §5):
    arrow syntax is accepted in component and provide-method bodies, but
    `infer_ir` types no arrow and no call through one there — checked at
    the service boundary, then silent inside. The G4 side is hardened
    (first-class emission references propagate `*`); the *typing* side
    remains open. Documented in function-types.md, but the standard this
    section sets is the errata, loudly — either close it or move the
    fence there.
  - nullary user-ADT constructors as values inside `test`-block expression
    scope — closed: a `test` body is the same expression scope a `fn` body
    is, so a bare `FirstTime` resolves as a value
    (`test_nullary_ctor_resolves_in_test_block` pins it).
- **Fence what stays open.** Anything not closed for a release gets a named
  entry in `docs/contract-errata.md` as a *known* unsoundness, with its trigger
  and blast radius, the way the cordis-rs A1 divergence already is. "Silent
  where unknown" is acceptable only when it is loud in the errata.

Exit test: no reachable program can violate a guarantee without either a
compile error or a documented errata entry.

### 2. `revl run`: one command, on a released runtime, any tier

**Status: roundtrip closed on py.** `revl run <manifest> --backend py` is a
first-class command (a654593, extended by `--placement` 8737253, replay's
`--record`, and the config-admission preflight): it compiles, boots the
composition on cordis-py with no host-language driver, streams the
load/lifecycle/host trace, and holds a live REPL over the provided services
(`:keys` to list, `:q` to quit; `--once` boots, tears down and exits for CI).
On EOF/Ctrl-C it unwinds LIFO and *proves* no residue (registry, provisions,
disposables, listeners back to baseline) before exiting. Config is a host
concern and is preflighted at admission — a component admitted with a missing
required config field refuses the run with a clear diagnostic, and the runtime
is loaded only after that check, so the diagnostic works with no cordis
installed. `--watch` is the file-watching hot-swap loop (demo/live.py
promoted to a first-class command): it recompiles on edit, refuses a rejected
or mis-hosted edit while keeping the running generation untouched, and swaps
in the new generation with derived LIFO teardown. `--placement` splits a
composition across processes. The end-to-end boot/exit contract is exercised
against the real runtime in `tests/test_run.py`; see also
docs/guide-humans.md (Tooling). A plain `python -m revl run
examples/user_cache.rvl --config cfg.toml < /dev/null` runs the whole
round-trip non-interactively.

Still open:

- [ ] the other tiers are not runnable — `--backend ts|rust|java|wasm` refuses
  loudly today (exit 2); each needs its runtime wired behind the same driver
  contract. The roadmap's multi-runtime exit test needs every tier to boot
  from the one command, not just py.
- [ ] pin cordis-py to a *tagged* release cut from `harden-fiber-lifecycle`
  (the R1-R5 documented contract): cordis-rs already targets 0.3.0 and
  cordis4j a source tag, so nobody builds the flagship tier against a branch.

Exit test: `git clone && revl run examples/...` prints a running composition on
every supported tier with no host-code edits.

### 3. Lighthouse workload: glitch agents (the self-deploying harness, DESIGN §1)

DESIGN §1's second motivation is a self-evolving agent harness: agents author
components, and revl is "the language such a harness could require its own
components to pass" before they self-deploy. **glitch agents** are the candidate
dogfood for exactly that. Running one real workload on revl is what turns
"research language" into "the language we run X on", and it will order the work
in 1 and 2 faster than any roadmap guess.

Shape to validate:
- agents emit `.rvl` components; `revl compile`/`audit` is the pre-deploy
  admission gate (reject means do not deploy, with the guarantee-naming
  diagnostic fed back to the generating agent);
- accepted components hot-swap into the running agent system via
  `revl run --watch`, with derived LIFO teardown on rollback so a bad
  generation reverts to no residue;
- the G8 boundary surface (externs, emissions) is the review surface for
  anything a generated component reaches outside its declared capabilities.

**Candidate answer (2026-08): DeepSeek Harness.** The first open question
below — what the harness runs on, and where revl slots in — now has a
concrete candidate. DSH (developer preview, 2026-08) is an agent harness
whose plugin kernel is Cordis itself: every capability, including the agent
loop, is a Cordis (TS) plugin, mounted and hot-swapped at runtime, and its
self-modification mode mounts *runtime-generated* plugins with no
pre-admission verification. That is precisely the gap this section
describes: revl slots in as the admission gate — the agent authors a `.rvl`
component, `compile`/`audit` admits or refuses it with the guarantee-naming
diagnostic, and an admitted component is emitted by the TS tier onto the
same runtime family DSH already runs on. What this sharpens:

- the target tier for this workload is **ts**, not py — which promotes the
  TS half of `revl run`'s "other tiers" item and gives item 14
  (`revl import cordis`) its consumer: a lighthouse composition will want
  to *require* an existing DSH plugin, not only replace one;
- a concrete first demo exists: orchestrate a real published DSH plugin
  (e.g. `dsh-ios`, a Cordis plugin with clear acquire/undo pairs —
  boot/shutdown, install/uninstall) from a revl composition, inject a
  failure mid-sequence, and show derived LIFO teardown leaving no residue;
- version reality check first: DSH pins its Cordis version — confirm the
  TS backend's emitted plugin shape loads on that pin before building
  anything else on this entry.

Still open:
- glitch agents remain the *authoring* side of the dogfood (who generates
  the components); DSH answers the *hosting* side. Whether the first
  lighthouse pairs them or starts DSH-only is unresolved;
- whether agents author whole components or fragments, which sets how much
  of `revl run --watch` is needed first.

### 4. Distribution & visibility, right-sized

A second team cannot build on a toolchain it cannot install. Right-sized
means: ship the cheap high-leverage pieces now, and *decline* the expensive
conventional ones that revl's shape does not actually need yet.

- **Publish the compiler to PyPI.** `pyproject.toml` (hatchling) already
  builds it; the missing step is a release workflow so `pip install revl`
  works from a clean machine. Cut releases from tags, and gate the publish
  on the same CI that gates main — a version on PyPI is a claim the suite
  passed.
- ✅ **A tree-sitter grammar.** The cheap, high-visibility slice of editor
  support: syntax highlighting in every tree-sitter host (GitHub, Neovim,
  Zed, Helix) for the cost of one grammar file, kept honest by parsing the
  same corpus the reference parser and selfhost/parser.rvl already agree
  on.
- **Explicitly deferred: a human-facing LSP.** For an agent-first language
  the MCP server *is* the language server — diagnostics, why-traces,
  in-memory compile, live-session state are already served to the client
  that writes most revl today. A conventional LSP duplicates that surface
  for a second client class and is worth building only when human authors
  are the bottleneck; record the decision here so its absence reads as a
  choice, not a gap.
- **The attention-readiness bundle — shipped *before* the item 18
  write-up goes out, not after.** The evidence items (18–20) are designed
  to generate inbound attention, and the repo currently has no front door
  for it. All cheap, all sequenced ahead of the post:
  - `SECURITY.md` — a project making safety claims without a disclosure
    path is a bad look on day one;
  - `CONTRIBUTING.md` — guide-ai-agents.md teaches agents to *use* revl,
    not to contribute to it; the wave/worktree/integration-branch
    workflow, the pre-commit contract, and the "every rejection joins the
    executable spec" rule live only in commit history today. Write it for
    both contributor kinds this repo actually gets: humans and agents;
  - issue templates — a bug report that arrives with the `.rvl` source,
    the tier, and the emitted output is triageable; one that arrives as
    prose is a day of archaeology;
  - a published **stability statement** — the frozen-v1 invariant is iron
    discipline internally but exists nowhere as a *promise*; state what
    is stable (v1 IR, byte-identical), what is versioned (ir_version
    tiers), and what may break without notice. Required before the PyPI
    publish above anyway — a version number on an index is implicitly
    this promise, so make it explicit.

## Declined, deliberately

Decisions against, consolidated so they read as choices and are not
relitigated item by item. Each lives in detail where cited:

- **The cordis4j global-realm fix** — errata'd, characterization-tested,
  revisited only if global realms on Java become load-bearing

### Reconsidered and reversed (kept on record so the change of direction is explicit, not the original stance)

- **Full self-hosting** — originally stopped at the differential-oracle
  boundary; later pursued and landed. The A to C to B arc reached a native
  `revl_compile`: `selfhost/lower.rvl` produces the full interchange IR
  natively (items 242, 241), and `selfhost/compile.rvl` compiles both
  function and component programs fully-native, byte-exact against the
  reference on py and rust with no reference in the chain (items 230, 262).
  The six tier emitters are self-hosted and cross-checked. What remains is
  native-tier performance (items 283, 284) and per-tier run coverage, not
  the self-hosting question itself.
- **A human-facing LSP** — originally deferred to the MCP server; later
  un-deferred and built as slice 1 (item 126): `src/revl/lsp/` over stdio,
  with diagnostics, hover, and go-to-definition.
  (docs/backends-roadmap.md).
- **wasm tier boundaries** (config blocks, host builtins, non-Int
  service types) — deliberate substrate-tier limits, each a precise
  EmitError; item 41's slices are the sanctioned path past them.
- **Mechanized metatheory** (Lean/Coq) — not pursued; the research claim
  is transfer-by-construction (docs/vision.md), and mechanization taxes
  the iteration speed that is the project's best asset. Revisit only
  alongside a paper submission that demands it.
- **Copying the workflow-engine signal pattern wholesale** — item 48
  states the mapping instead; only durable delivery and event
  subscription are real gaps.
- **Sandboxing extern host code in-place** — item 24's threat-model
  non-goal; item 45's quarantine tier is the architectural answer.
- **A component repository's *scale tier* before key namespacing** —
  re-scoped 2026-08: the collision argument gates publishing at scale
  (item 49 phase 2, still behind item 9's namespacing), not the
  read/reuse path — G2 already refuses colliding providers at
  admission, so phase 0 (structural search + fetch + reuse hints) is
  unblocked and prioritized. The demand test ("a second author")
  survives as phase 2's gate; the second *agent* author is already
  here.
- **A first-class `stream` type** — the WIT importer already took the
  stance (docs/import-wit.md §2): `stream<T>` is an open-ended effect,
  not a value — model it as a service whose operations are `emission`.
  Streams-as-services keeps every guarantee's surface intact; a native
  stream type would need its own lifecycle story on six tiers for a
  construct the service model already expresses. Revisit only if item
  41's Component Model work makes wire-level streams unavoidable.
- **Placement discovery/orchestration/scheduling** — item 56 stops at
  static placement maps over machines. revl is not becoming an
  orchestrator; it composes with one.

## Invariants (checked every step)

- v1 goldens byte-identical; frontend/backend-py/backend-ts suites green
  (currently 200 / 21 / 24).
- Every new rejection lands in `examples/rejections/` and joins the executable
  spec (`tests/test_frontend.py` REJECTIONS).
- Error messages are a deliverable (DESIGN §9): each names its guarantee and
  the fix — and is written for the model that will read it.


## Follow-ups from harness findings (items 84/85)

86. ✅ **Complete host `Map` iteration (`keys()`/`size()`) on ts/rust/java.** Item 84 fixed the py host `Map` runtime; the same gap exists on ts (`MapHandle`), rust (`struct Map<V>`), and java (`class Map<V>`) — their host `Map` carries only `new/insert/remove/get/drop`, so `store.keys()`/`store.size()` type-check clean (host-receiver provenance isn't tracked in provide bodies) but have no runtime backing and crash at run. wasm is safe (rejects `Map.new` at compile time). Mirror 84: add `keys()` (sorted canonical Str order) and `size()` to each tier's host `Map` runtime, pinned by a compile→emit→run test per tier. Do NOT add `values()`/`items()` — not part of the promised Map surface (docs/stdlib-2.0.md).

87. ✅ **Teach `selfhost/lexer.rvl` about triple-quoted strings (`"""`).** Item 85 added `"""..."""` to `src/revl/lexer.py`; the self-hosted lexer (`selfhost/lexer.rvl`, cross-checked token-for-token by `test_selfhost_lexer.py`) does not yet know the form. It passes today only because no corpus `.rvl` file uses `"""` — the moment self-hosting authors a triple-quoted source (the whole point of 85), the cross-check diverges. Add the same `"""` handling to `selfhost/lexer.rvl`.

88. ✅ **Complete host `Map` iteration (`keys()`/`size()`) on the go tier.** Items 84/86 fixed py/ts/rust/java; the go host `Map` (`type Map struct` in `backends/go/emit.py`) still carries only New/Insert/Remove/Get/Drop — same latent gap (`store.keys()`/`store.size()` type-check clean but have no runtime backing). Mirror 84/86: add `Keys()` (sorted canonical code-point order) and `Size()` to the go host Map runtime, pinned by a compile→run test. wasm remains n/a (rejects `Map.new` at compile time). This closes host-Map iteration across all six tiers.
91. ✅ **Lower declared function types on the rust tier (harness multi-tier
    proof, finding #20).** The harness's agent loop is a top-level fn over
    effectful callback arrows (`agent_loop(msgs, complete:
    (List[Msg]) -> Str, call_tool: (Str, Str) -> Str, max_steps)`). On
    py and ts this lowers and runs (FR-1's pattern); on rust the emitter
    refuses the whole document: "a declared function type is not
    lowerable on the Rust tier" (docs/function-types.md §5 — rust wants
    `impl Fn(..)` for a param, `Box<dyn Fn(..)>` for an escaping value,
    and revl carries no position distinction). So the string-protocol
    harness (no JSON, no externs — the "runs on all runtimes" proof)
    boots on py/ts but not rust, solely because of this declaration.
    Arrows bound to a local `let` and called in the same function still
    lower (rustc infers the closure type), so the fix is position-aware:
    thread the *call-site* knowledge (param vs local vs field) into the
    rust emitter's fn-type lowering, choosing `impl Fn`/`Box<dyn Fn>` per
    position, or beta-reduce single-use arrow params at the call site as
    the java tier already does. The harness keeps the mtier variant
    (`mtier/`) as the executable repro.
90. ✅ **Item-80 phase 2: let module fns carry the async color (harness
    verification of slice 1+2+3).** Slices 1-3 landed on
    `agent/async-extern-slice1` and were verified against the harness:
    the parser accepts `extern emission async fn`, the A1 coloring refuses
    a sync method reaching an async extern (with the witness chain), and
    the ts emitter produces `async function http_post(...): Promise<string>`
    with awaited call sites that pass `tsc --noEmit` — the harness's
    `Promise<string>` vs `string` blocker is gone. The remaining gap is
    the design's phase-2 rule: a *module fn* body reaching an async extern
    is refused ("cannot carry the async color yet", test_module_fn_...
    refused_phase2). This blocks the harness's agent loop, which funnels
    `model.complete` (async) through the top-level `agent_loop` callback
    arrows — the direct path (`async fn run` calling `emit model.complete`
    inline) compiles and typechecks, but the loop shape needs module fns
    to propagate the color (a fixpoint, as the design §3 table reserves).
    When it lands, the harness's `agent_loop` works unchanged.
92. ✅ **Async function values: a callback of sync function type cannot
    carry the async color (harness verification of item 90, finding
    #21).** Item 90's transitive coloring works for *direct* module-fn
    calls, but the harness's loop passes the async call through a
    callback arrow: `agent_loop(msgs, complete: (List[Msg]) -> Str, ...)`
    with `msgs => emit model.complete(msgs)` at the call site. The
    emitted loop stays sync (`resp = complete(current)` — no await, both
    py and ts), and the arrow returns a coroutine/Promise that leaks to
    `decode_reply`/`json_parse` at runtime ("the JSON object must be
    str..., not coroutine"). Item 90's roadmap text said "the harness's
    `agent_loop` works unchanged" — it does not: the function type
    `(List[Msg]) -> Str` has no async variant, so the fixpoint cannot
    color the callback, and the call site's arrow (which reaches an async
    op) is a sync-typed value. The 90 claim is falsified by execution.
    Fix directions: (a) an async-aware function type
    (`(…) -> Async[Str]` or a `Promise`-style return), so a callback
    whose body reaches an async extern is typed async and the loop awaits
    it; (b) checker-inferred arrow coloring — when an arrow's body
    reaches an async extern, mark the arrow async and require await at
    its use sites (the fixpoint extended to first-class values); or (c)
    the loop declares `complete: (List[Msg]) -> Async[Str]` explicitly.
    Executable repro: the harness's async-migration branch
    (`src/components/agent.rvl`, `agent_loop`), where the mock+loop
    lifecycle tests fail with the coroutine leak while the direct path
    passes.
93. ✅ **Emitted rust for the loop harness has three compile bugs (harness
    multi-tier proof, finding #22).** Item 91's fn-type lowering works
    (the `impl Fn` params emit), but `revl run --backend rust --once` on
    the string-protocol harness fails `cargo build` with 7 errors, three
    distinct emitter bugs:
    (a) **`config` is not in scope in provide-method bodies on rust** —
    `self.sessions.load(config.session_id.clone())` emits `config` with no
    binding (E0425); the rust emitter does not thread the component config
    into provide-method scope the way py/ts do. Minimal shape:
    `component C { config { name: Str } provide s { fn get() =
    config.name } }`.
    (b) **persistent `push` rebinding in a loop moves the value** — the
    loop's `current = current.revl_push(...)` emits `revl_push` taking
    `self` by value, so the RHS consumes `current` before the assignment
    rebinds (E0382 use of moved value). Persistent `push`/`concat` need a
    borrow (`&self`) or a clone in the rebind position.
    (c) **non-Copy ADT and `impl Fn` params used twice** — `is_tool(dec)`
    then `exec_reply(dec, ...)` consume `dec` (non-Copy `ModelReply`), and
    `call_tool: impl Fn(...)` is moved into a call inside the loop; both
    need clones or reference-taking calls.
    Repro: `revl run mtier/*.rvl --backend rust --once` in the harness
    repo (mtier/ is the string-protocol multi-tier proof). Fixes are
    local to backends/rust/emit.py.

94. ✅ **Async function-value erasure leaks on the go and rust tiers (follow-up to item 92).** Item 92 landed async fn values on py/ts; go/rust ERASE the color but render the function-type return wrong: **go** emits `complete func(string) Async[Str]` (invalid Go — `go build` rejects it), **rust** renders the erased async return as `Value` instead of the concrete type (`impl Fn(String) -> Value`). Both are one-line emitter fixes: strip/erase the `Async[T]` wrapper to `T` in each tier fn-type rendering. Conformance is currently green only because the corpus does not exercise an async-typed callback on go/rust; pin a test that does.

95. ✅ **Str and record lowering on the wasm tier — ALREADY DELIVERED by the v3 linear-memory wave (b79a047/e3f9733/c8fc107).** Filed off stale roadmap prose; verify-first found the wasm tier already lowers Str/records/lists/variants over a linear-memory ABI (ptr+len strings, offset-slot records), executing on wasmtime (backends/wasm 33 passed). Still refused (documented): Float, Map, fn-values, split/join, config blocks. The real remaining wasm-bridge work is item 41 slice-3 (map this custom layout onto the standard WASI-P2 canonical ABI: cabi_realloc, WIT-typed exports, string lift/lower) — no longer gated on "wasm cannot represent Str/records".

96. ✅ **wasm `_V3Emitter` emits invalid wasm for a function whose whole body is a diverging `if`/`else` statement** (missing trailing `unreachable`) — found during 41-s3 aggregates; affects the NORMAL wasm tier, not just the canonical path. `backends/wasm/emit.py`. Bounded fix.

97. ✅ **`export_wit` emits referenced `record`/`variant` types at the WIT package top level** (invalid WIT — must be inside an `interface`); found during 41-s3 aggregates, worked around locally with a relocate helper. `src/revl/export_wit.py`. Bounded fix.

98. ✅ (fixed via item 150) **Cross-suite pytest isolation: tests/ + backends/wasm/ share golden/temp artifacts.** Running both in ONE pytest process yields ~11 backends/wasm/test_canonical_abi.py failures (reproduces on clean main; each suite passes alone). Not a CI risk today (separate CI jobs), but a real isolation smell (shared temp/golden state). Low priority. Found during items 96/97.

99. ✅ **Timers on go/rust/wasm (follow-on to item 57).** Item 57 landed every/after timers as revertible effects on py+ts; go/rust/wasm refuse honestly. Implement the schedule/cancel + clock-coeffect contract (docs/time-coeffect.md) on go and rust (Clock + TimerHandle mirroring py/ts, unload cancels residue-free). wasm timer emit remains a further follow-on (behind 41 slice-3 service-level).

104. ✅ **`Str.length` (PROPERTY form) on a multibyte string literal returns the UTF-8 byte count, not code points — REAL BUG (earlier not-a-bug call was wrong).** `fn M() -> Int { return "café".length }` const-folds to `(i64.extend_i32_u (i32.load (i32.const 0)))` = byte-len prefix (5), executes to 5 on wasmtime (should be 4). The `.length()` METHOD form is correct (routes through `$str_cp_length`) — my earlier verify tested the method form and missed the property fast-path. Fix the property `.length` fast-path (route through `$str_cp_length` or fold the compile-time code-point count); audit `charAt`/`charCodeAt`/`slice`/`indexOf` property fast-paths for the same byte-vs-code-point fold; reconcile docs/strings.md (line ~52 vs ~263 contradiction). `backends/wasm/emit.py`.

105. ✅ **Remove the dead `_relocate_types_into_interface` workaround in backends/wasm/canonical.py.** Item 97 (ec97d07) made `export_wit` emit named types INSIDE the interface (valid WIT) — confirmed on main. The canonical component builder's local relocate transform (canonical.py:685, used ~880) is now redundant; remove it and update `test_aggregate_wit_is_valid_types_inside_interface` to assert export_wit output directly. Keep only if removal breaks the canonical build (report why).

106. ✅ **Re-filed from stranded branch `agent/harness-m3` (never merged; its item numbers 98/99 were later reused on main): async colour does not propagate through spawned-handle emissions in arrow bodies (harness finding #27).** The async-colour analysis (`src/revl/emission_analysis.py::_async_callables`, call-graph based) sees `req`-based emissions (`model.complete` on a `requires`) but not handle-based ones (`w1.wtask.run` on a spawned instance), so an arrow whose body is `emit <handle>.<key>.<method>()` is not flagged async and the py emitter wraps it with `_revl_as_async` (the sync classification) — `await run_one(task)` then yields a coroutine OBJECT ("can only concatenate str (not 'coroutine') to str", "coroutine was never awaited"). Fix: treat `handle.key.method()` like `req.key.method()` in the colour analysis (the spawn handle's service type is known); defensively, `_revl_as_async` should `return await _f(*a)` when `_f` returns a coroutine. Full write-up in branch commits e2b1b27/c92e78e; also cherry-pick `dogfood/findings-timers-tiers.md` from c92e78e, which never landed.

107. ✅ **Re-filed from `agent/harness-m3` (finding #28) — REPRODUCED on main 2026-08-24: a `let`/`var` declared type annotation is dropped in the IR, so the wasm tier cannot type an empty list initializer.** `var out: List[Int] = []` type-checks (the checker sees the annotation) and lowers on py/ts, but `revl test --backend wasm` fails with `emitter refused: an untyped empty list literal needs an expected List type`. The annotation exists at check time and is discarded before emission; carry the declared type on the let/var IR node (or pin the empty-literal type from it, as `_pin_empty_literal` does for other positions) so the wasm emitter sees the expected List type. Original analysis in branch commit 63f44a0. `src/revl/lower.py`, `backends/wasm/emit.py`.

108. ✅ **Packaging: the published wheel only supports compile/audit/mcp — every backend-touching command breaks when installed from PyPI.** The wheel packs `src/revl` alone (pyproject `packages = ["src/revl"]`), but `revl run` (all tiers), `revl test` on non-py tiers, placement, and fault tests resolve emitters and runtimes via `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2] / "backends"` (src/revl/run.py:740, fault.py:44, run_go.py:63, run_java.py:64, run_wasm.py:57, _process_runner.py:94) — a path that does not exist under site-packages. Also no `[project.scripts]`: `pip install revl` yields no `revl` command while README and most docs write bare `revl compile …` (only `python -m revl` works). Fine from a checkout; broken as a package. Ship the emitters inside the wheel (package data or a `revl.backends` subpackage) with a checkout-first fallback, and add a console script. GATES the PyPI release (the publish workflow exists and would ship this today).

109. ✅ **Stale runnable-backend prose + dead refusal branch.** All six tiers are in `RUNNABLE_BACKENDS` (run_ts/run_go wired), but: `--backend` help still says "ts and go emit but have no run driver yet" (src/revl/__main__.py:1681); README line ~223 still says `--backend {py,rust,java,wasm}` with "ts refused"; and since `KNOWN_BACKENDS == RUNNABLE_BACKENDS` the refusal branch at src/revl/run.py:699–705 and the "(not runnable yet)" suffix at run.py:151 are unreachable — delete them or keep RUNNABLE as the single source the help text renders from. Also: this file's header still says "**Branch:** `v2.0`" though the work lands on main.

110. **Repo hygiene sweep (from the 2026-08-24 deep review).** (a) `.srv.log` — a localhost HTTP access log — is committed at the repo root; delete and gitignore. (b) 49 of 51 local branches are fully merged — prune; ~24 worktrees live in /private/tmp and dangle after a reboot (`git worktree prune`). (c) The committed playground wheel (site/vendor) is one src commit stale (built at f2aa09f; src/revl changed in 2c929a2) and nothing checks it — add a CI freshness gate that rebuilds via `site/build.py` and diffs, or regenerate on src change. (d) 202 `noqa` comments carry ruff codes but no ruff config or CI job exists — pin ruff (F-level currently finds ~35 trivia: 9 unused imports, 6 unused locals e.g. lower.py:4878 `lines`, go/emit.py:1638–1639 `sig_arg`/`call_arg`, 20 placeholder-less f-strings, and a string-annotation `"Env"` undefined at emission_analysis.py:315) and add the job so the existing noqa discipline is enforced.

111. ✅ **Refactor the largest compiler units (low priority, no behaviour change).** `main()` is 889 lines (src/revl/__main__.py:1230) — split into per-subcommand handlers; `_lower_provide` (395 lines), `_lower_component_pure_expr` (351), `_lower_pure_expr` (298), `check_and_lower` (296) in the 4.9k-line src/revl/lower.py factor into per-statement/expression-kind helpers; wasm `_V3Emitter` is a single 2,568-line class (backends/wasm/emit.py). Large expr dispatchers are conventional for compilers — the bar is "each helper testable alone", not a line count. Golden/conformance suites are the safety net; land as mechanical moves, one unit per PR.

112. ✅ **`advance` lifecycle statement fails hard on go/rust lifecycle emitters** (re-filed from agent/harness-m3 finding #29; verification of item 102). Item 102 landed `advance <n><unit>` on py/ts; `revl test --backend rust|go` on the same doc fails `emitter refused: unknown lifecycle step 'advance'` — a HARD FAIL, not an honest `_timer_follow_on`/`_lifecycle_refusal` skip. Item 99 gave go/rust timers, so `advance` should lower on go/rust (drive their Clock) or at minimum skip honestly like timers do. `backends/go/emit.py`, `backends/rust/emit.py` (lifecycle emitters).

113. ✅ **go host `Map` is `map[string]string` only — `Map[Str, Int]` silently mis-lowers to invalid go** (re-filed from agent/harness-m3 finding #30; verification of item 99). `backends/go/emit.py`'s host Map is `type Map struct { m map[string]string }` — string values only. The harness's counters (`Map[Str, Int]`) and ledgers (`Map[Str, List[Msg]]`) emit `Get`/`Insert` against it and `go test` fails `cannot use _v (string) as int64`. Never surfaced until item 99 made go v3 docs build. Carry the actual value type in the go host Map lowering. `backends/go/emit.py`.

114. ✅ **item 101's rust clone fix missed the `_Env` provide-method renderer — a local reused after an emit still moves** (re-filed from agent/harness-m3 finding #31; verification of item 101). `_method_body_lines` clones only PARAMS into the acquire rename (`acquire_rename[param] = param.clone()`), never LOCALS, so `emit sessions.append(sid, Msg { content: answer })` then `return answer` (where `answer` is a `let`-bound local) renders `content: answer` bare and moves (E0382). Item 101 fixed `_V3Ctx` (record fields + direct service args) but not the `_Env` provide-method path. Extend the clone to reused locals in the `_Env` renderer. `backends/rust/emit.py`.

115. ✅ **py erases async externs to blocking `def`s, so a component cannot `await` a host operation** (re-filed from agent/harness-m3 finding #32). `_emit_externs` (`backends/python/emit.py`) emits EVERY extern as a blocking `def`, and the py await-seed excludes externs (emit.py:97-99). An async extern whose host body wants to `await` (dispose a fiber, wait on a promise) is inexpressible on py; ts handles it. Emit async externs as `async def` and let the await-seed include them on py. Collides with items 106/106-family on `backends/python/emit.py` — sequence after. `backends/python/emit.py`.

116. ✅ **`revl import cordis` cannot see DSH's real plugin shapes** (re-filed from agent/harness-m3 finding #33). Three gaps, DSH's real plugins as the corpus: (a) surface recovery matches only `extends (?:Name\.)*Service` — misses Service subclasses with a non-`Service` base name; (b) decorated methods are invisible; (c) named record types across local imports are not transcribed. The cordis importer. Each independently useful.

117. ✅ **A1/coloring is blind to async ops in EXPRESSION positions — a sync context reaching an async op is neither refused nor colored (subsumes harness finding #40; found in item 106).** The A1/async-coloring analysis (`lower.py::_req_op_is_async`/`_arrow_reaches_async`, `emission_analysis.py`) only sees async ops in statement position, not nested in a ternary or an arrow body. Two manifestations: (a) an UNCOLORED arrow whose body reaches an async op (item 106); (b) finding #40 — a SYNC provide method whose ternary returns an async emission (`fn route(p) { return p=="go" ? emit m.complete(p) : "idle" }`) is admitted, and ts emits a sync `route` returning a Promise that the lifecycle driver never awaits -> the assertion sees `{}` (py passes by accident). Per A1 a sync method has NO in-flight window, so the CHECKER should REFUSE sync-reaching-async in any expression position (ternary/arrow), forcing `async` (which item 141 then awaits correctly). Complements item 141 (async-side await). Cam worked around it in web_shell (A1-correct async dispatch, 16/16 py+ts) — this is the soundness fix, not a blocker. Frontend: `lower.py`/`emission_analysis.py`/`typecheck.py`. Queue AFTER item 141.

## Post-v2.0 horizon — strategy review 2026-08-24

Curated from the 2026-08-24 strategy review. **Excluded as already built:** TCP+mTLS
bridge transport (item 56), Wasm sandboxing for generated code (item 45, quarantine
tier), `checked_div_*` total division (item 13). **Excluded as in-progress:** the
Automorphing Harness / agent loop (revl scaffold, revl learn, revl evolve,
confidence-gated admission) — actively being built elsewhere, do not re-file.
These items are horizon work, not the current wave; each notes what it builds on.

### Distributed execution
118. **`revl deploy` — composition as a distributed deployment.** Extend `apply` to remote hosts (SSH/container per component), bridge over TCP (item 56), WAL replicated across nodes, LIFO rollback on partial failure. Builds on `apply.py`/`distribute.py`/`placement.py`/`recovery.py`. ~2-3 wk. Novelty: verified deployment with LIFO rollback on partial failure. 

    **+ attested supply-chain deploy (external proposal #7, reconcile here not a new item).** Link source -> IR hash -> backend-artifact hash -> capability policy -> gauntlet evidence -> signature -> deployment -> runtime trace, building on attest.py (item 127, done), truc, federation, mTLS, and the item-33 policy gate. A remote host REJECTS an artifact if: its source or IR hash changed, its backend differs from the attestation, its capability set expanded, its policy is incompatible, its evidence is stale, or its signer is untrusted. Gives `revl deploy` a cryptographically-linked identity: deploy only artifacts whose source, guarantees, capabilities, and runtime target are all linked. 

    **+ transactional multi-composition federation update (external proposal #5, folded here).** `revl apply federation.plan` commits an atomic change across several compositions (replace model in A, migrate router in B, update policy in C) ONLY IF every composition admits + network contracts stay compatible + no capability surface expands + state handoff succeeds + all health probes pass; otherwise the WHOLE federation returns to its previous generation. All-or-nothing distributed control plane.
 

    **+ distributed effect correlation (external proposal #6, folded here).** Every effect crossing a TCP+mTLS boundary carries a common identity (composition_id, generation, realm, effect_id, idempotency_key, parent_effect) so a remote provider correlates the chain for recovery, OTel (120/121), duplicate detection, cross-process teardown, and incident investigation.

    **+ STRATEGIC NOTE (external-reviewer batch, 2026-08-26).** An external reviewer's whole 'productionization' cluster - reproducible bundle, migration planner, effect correlation, production fault-injection, SLO contracts, resource-aware placement - is DOWNSTREAM of THIS item's remote transport. Build 118's remote parts (cross-machine mTLS, replicated WAL, distributed rollback) FIRST; the production-ops layer stands on it. It is the single highest-leverage unlock for that batch.
 

    **+ 253 is the cheap distributed ALTERNATIVE to this item (Fable).** Item 253 (Temporal emission target) gives durable/distributed/compensating execution on someone else's cluster as an emit-target variant, with NO transport layer to build - so if distribution pressure arrives before 118's remote transport is affordable, 253 is the answer, which strengthens the case that 118 is safely deferrable.

119. ✅ **Capability/realm-aware placement optimization.** `placement.py` places components on backends (which tier); extend to place on HOSTS by capability, realm, topology, resource (`emission[database]` near the db; `realm("eu")` in the EU). Compile-time-verified deployment constraint. Builds on `placement.py`/`plan.py`/realm+capability systems. ~2-3 wk. High novelty.

### Observability & production ops 

    **+ resource-aware placement (external proposal #14, folded here).** Extend placement beyond backend+host with resource requirements (memory <= 64MiB, cpu <= 1 core, latency_to = database.eu, wasm = required, network = forbidden); the planner REFUSES an incompatible placement before deployment ('component CsvReader requires wasm isolation; selected host exposes no compatible wasm runtime'). Connects the capability/realm system to embedded/edge/cloud. Relates to wasm runtime limits (289).

120. ✅ (LANDED on main via 615c8a9 + extended by item 159 schema v2; src/revl/otel.py [498 lines] maps causal lifecycle traces to OTel spans/events/links, test_otel.py green. 🚧 marker was stale; obsolete older branch agent/otel-integration superseded) **OpenTelemetry integration.** Map the causal lifecycle traces (`why_runtime.py`) to OTel spans/events/links (lifecycle transition = span, effect = event, emission = linked span) so revl compositions appear in Grafana/Datadog/Honeycomb/Jaeger. Format mapping, not new semantics. ~1 wk. High adoption value. 

    **+ proof-carrying telemetry (external proposal #15, folded here).** Extend the emitted OTel spans to carry verification context: trace_id, component, generation, source_hash, attestation_hash, capability, policy, lifecycle. Then an incident answers in one place: which exact source ran, which capabilities it had, which policy admitted it, which gauntlet evidence existed, what changed from the previous generation, could it be replayed. Ties otel + attest (127) + diff (123) + recovery + audit into one operational story.

121. **`revl trace` — distributed tracing with LLM hops.** Extend causal traces with LLM fields per agent decision (prompt, model, token count, cost, latency, confidence), linked to the emission it produced and the G-rule that verified it. Builds on `why_runtime.py`/`why.py`/replay timeline. ~1 wk. Novelty: LLM-aware tracing with compile-time-verification context.
122. ✅ **`revl metrics` — capability-aware runtime metrics.** Per composition: emission count by capability, failure count by G-rule, avg lifecycle duration, residue incidents, admission rejections by cause — guarantee-mapped SLIs ("G4 violations: 0 this week"). Builds on the interchange format, `audit_diff.py`, the runtime trace ledger. ~1-2 wk.

### Tooling & ecosystem
123. ✅ (LANDED on main via 96a638f; src/revl/composition_diff.py [306 lines] + `revl diff` subcommand wired, IR-level structural delta over audit_diff foundation, test_composition_diff.py 13/13 green. 🚧 marker was stale; branch agent/revl-diff is a byte-identical duplicate) **`revl diff` — semantic composition diff.** Given two compositions, show the IR-level structural delta (components added/removed/changed, capabilities introduced/removed, deps added/broken) — "component X gained `emission[bus]`", not "line 14 changed". The PR-review tool for agent-generated code. Builds on `audit_diff.py` (already the foundation). ~1 wk. 

    **+ multi-version components checked against ACTUAL consumers (external proposal #3, folded here).** A registry runs several contract versions at once (cache@1/@2/@3); compute which consumers can migrate, which adapters are needed, whether both coexist without a G2 conflict, whether migration adds capabilities, and whether the old version is removable safely. More precise than semver because compatibility is checked against actual consumers + realms, not a version string (uses this diff + item 9 versioning).
 

    **+ verified migration planner (external proposal #2, folded here).** `revl migrate old.json new.json --policy production.toml` classifies each change safe / requires-handoff / canary / requires-approval / refused, and reports required state handoff, affected consumers, capability changes, canary requirements, rollback feasibility, and whether the old generation is safely removable. Makes hot-swap production-useful, not just technically possible. Builds on this diff + state-handoff (53) + swap (23).

124. ✅ **`revl profile` — capability/emission profiling + over-declaration detection.** Profile which declared capabilities are actually used and how often; flag over-declaration (declares `emission[db,bus,email]`, only uses `db`) and suggest tightening. G4 bounds from above; this tightens from below — least authority as a precision tool. Builds on the runtime trace ledger + capability system. ~1-2 wk.
125. **Cross-tier fault sweep (`revl test --backend all --sweep`).** Extend the reference-tier fault sweep to all six tiers — fault at every step, on every runtime, assert identical residue-free cleanup. The strongest A8 test: "no residue on ANY tier, and they all agree." Builds on `fault.py` (sweep), the cross-tier execution harness, the run_* drivers. ~1-2 wk. Novel testing methodology. 

    **+ deterministic fault injection in production-like environments (external proposal #7, folded here).** A `[faults]` profile (provider_death=0.01, network_timeout=0.02, after_effect_before_inverse_record=0.001, mTLS_disconnect=0.01) run under controlled injection, requiring residue-free + a known recovery verdict + 0 unclassified failures. Complements this exhaustive sweep with probabilistic prod-like injection. Relates to item 295 (concurrency testing).

126. ✅ (SLICE 1 — new src/revl/lsp/ package [protocol/server/analysis/document], hand-rolled Content-Length JSON-RPC over stdio, `python -m revl.lsp`; publishDiagnostics via compile_source+diagnostics.classify, hover via diagnostics.explain/GUARANTEES/FIXES, go-to-def via a parser-AST SymbolTable; NO edit to any reused module. 17 tests + live subprocess. Follow-on slices: column-precise spans, code-actions, incremental sync) **`revl` human-facing LSP — DECIDED: BUILD (un-deferred).** The MCP server (`src/revl/mcp/`) remains the primary agent-facing language server for the client that writes most revl (the deferral rationale still holds for THAT audience); this item adds a HUMAN-facing LSP for editor adoption, additive and disjoint. Slice 1: a stdio LSP (minimal Content-Length JSON-RPC, no new third-party dep) reusing the existing surfaces read-only — publishDiagnostics via the checker + `diagnostics.classify`/`GUARANTEES`, hover via `diagnostics.explain`/`GUARANTEES`/`FIXES` + symbol info, go-to-definition via the resolver, over `typecheck.py`/`diagnostics.py`/`why.py`/`parser.py` + `tree-sitter-revl/`. Code-actions/refactors and incremental sync -> a follow-on slice. NEW `src/revl/lsp/` package, no edits to the reused modules.** The roadmap deliberately deferred a human-facing LSP ("the MCP server IS the agent-facing language server"). But `tree-sitter-revl/` + `typecheck.py` + `diagnostics.py` + `why.py` all exist. Reconsider for HUMAN developer adoption (inline diagnostics/hover/go-to-def/code-actions in editors). ~2-3 wk. The decision to build or keep-deferred is the item.

### Security & safety
127. ✅ **`revl attest` — cryptographic attestation of verified compositions.** After the gauntlet verifies, emit a signed attestation (timestamp, passes G2-G8/A8, these capabilities, source hash) via sigstore or a keypair — cryptographic proof for regulated environments (banking/healthcare/gov). Builds on the interchange format, gauntlet, audit. ~1-2 wk. Compliance angle.
128. ✅ **Prompt-injection resistance — framing/documentation (effort ~0, it already works).** Capability confinement already means a generated component cannot exceed its declared emissions regardless of what a prompt injection says — G4 rejects at compile time (language-level, not prompt-level, defense). Articulate and document this guarantee. Builds on G4 + the agent generation loop. Documentation, not a build.

### Language features
129. **Closures over mutable state — decide and document.** Closures capture by value only (syntax-2.0 §3.5). Decide explicitly: refuse mutable capture with a clear diagnostic (likely — it breaks the value-semantic equality G7 relies on) OR support it and specify its LIFO-teardown interaction. The decision + the G7-interaction analysis is the item. ~1 wk. 

    **+ this is a genuine DECISION, not just documentation (Fable): refuse mutable capture with a diagnostic, OR support it and specify the G7/A8 interaction. Likely outcome refuse+diagnostic; the G7-interaction analysis is the deliverable.**

130. **`Stream[T]` reactive types.** A verified reactive sequence: a stream provider emits values over time, a consumer processes them as they arrive; provider teardown closes the stream, consumer teardown releases the subscription (LIFO). The language-level form of reactive coeffects for event-driven sagas (Kafka/webhooks/real-time). Needs its own lifecycle story on six tiers. Builds on the reactive coeffect model + provide/inject graph. ~3-4 wk. High novelty. 

    **v1 design spec (from an external proposal, verified: no Stream type exists yet).** Do NOT start with a general reactive calculus. v1 = `Stream[T]` + a small API (`subscribe`/`next`/`close`/`map`/`filter`/`take`/`merge`) and an `every x in subscription { ... }` async-iteration form. Support ONLY: one consumer, explicit `close`, bounded buffering, a deterministic test-time clock, documented refusals for unsupported tiers/shapes; NO implicit multicast, NO automatic retry. Model the subscription as an `acquire`/`undo` resource with an optional state type `Stream[T, State]` (Created->Active->Paused->Closed). CORE GUARANTEE: every admitted stream subscription has an explicit cancellation path, and unloading its owner closes the stream before the owner disappears (a natural extension of acquire/undo + G5/G7/A8 LIFO teardown). Hard-parts to DECIDE before implementing: single-consumer vs multicast (start single), cancellation ownership (the subscription owner), provider-death behavior (consumer becomes Pending or gets a terminal event), backpressure (bounded buffer + explicit policy), replay (only if the provider declares it), the common minimal six-tier protocol (wasm has no async, so it REFUSES streams like it refuses timers), effectful callbacks (allowed but capability+lifecycle checked), cross-realm (only via an explicit bridge), crash recovery (recreate the subscription or mark it non-reconstructible). Blast radius: parser + typecheck + lower + all six backends + async-coloring (A1/106). Design-first, ~3-4 wk. 

    **+ typed external EVENTS (external proposal #10, folded here).** A first-class event contract (`event OrderCreated { order_id: Str, timestamp: Int }`, `on OrderCreated as event { ... }`) with the checker verifying schema compatibility, consumer lifecycle, duplicate handling, event ownership, replay behavior, handler idempotency, and that a failed handler does not leave a subscription active. Related to this Stream[T] item (an event is a stream item with a contract); decide whether events are a distinct surface or a Stream specialization. Useful for queues, MCP notifications, WebSockets, IoT.

131. **Explicit async/await effect composition (surface syntax).** Async EXTERNS already land (items 80/90/92/106/115) and the runtime supports async bodies; this is the remaining piece — explicit language-level async/await for EFFECT composition (a provider doing I/O awaits without blocking the consumer's coeffect activation), with verified LIFO teardown. Scope carefully vs. what async externs already cover. ~2-3 wk. Novelty: async with verified LIFO teardown.

### Academic formalization
132. **Mechanized soundness proof (Lean/Coq).** Formalize G7 (LIFO), A8 (no residue), G4 (capability containment) as a type/effect system with a soundness proof. The paper that turns the engineering into a proven contribution. ~3-6 mo.
133. **Cross-tier agreement theorem.** Formalize the conditions under which six runtimes produce identical values for the same IR (operands annotation, Int/Float, code-point Str unit, canonical Map order) and prove the lowering preserves semantics across tiers. Builds on the conformance matrix + DIVERGENCES table. ~2-4 mo. No prior art.

134. ✅ **`revl import cordis` still refuses DSH's REAL gateway — external `Service`-named bases and `.ts`-extension imports (re-filed from agent/harness-m3 finding #34; follow-on to item 116).** Item 116 recovered in-file base chains, decorated methods, and extensionless local imports, but the real `PluginInventoryGateway` (`extends TypertRemoteService` imported from `@deepseek-ai/dsh-typert-protocol`, and `import type { X } from './types.ts'` WITH the `.ts` extension) still reports "exposes no method surface this importer can read". Two gaps: (a) `_service_class`'s `chain_of` only walks LOCAL classes; treat a non-local base whose name ends in `Service` (the cordis convention) as a service root. (b) resolve local imports that carry the `.ts` extension. `src/revl/import_cordis.py`.

135. ✅ **rust emitter's `\u{...}` non-ASCII escape collides with `format!()` placeholders (re-filed from agent/harness-m3 finding #35; verification of items 112-114).** `_string` (`backends/rust/emit.py`:~382) escapes a non-ASCII scalar (e.g. em dash U+2014 in a test name) as `\u{2014}`; in a format-string position (`assert!`, `format!`) Rust parses the `{2014}` inside the escape as a positional-arg reference → `invalid reference to positional argument 2014`. Fix: emit literal UTF-8 for non-surrogate scalars (Rust source is UTF-8 — the escape is only needed for the lone-surrogate case) OR escape braces in format positions. Repro: any rust doc with a non-ASCII char in a string that lands in an assert/format position. `backends/rust/emit.py`.

136. ✅ **`truc` — the revl component manager (name decided 2026-08-24; horizon: Tooling & ecosystem).** The component/package manager is named **truc** (lowercase); identity and vocabulary in docs/truc.md — components are *petits bouts* assembled into a composition, so truc *assembles*, it does not "install". First loop: `truc add`/`rm` (pull a component + manifest into the composition), `truc assemble` (resolve → admit (G2) → compose), `truc ship` (publish), `truc.toml` + `truc.lock`, vendored components in `trucs/`; dual entry point (standalone `truc` + `revl add` alias). Builds on `registry/` — index.json already carries provides/requires surfaces, manifestHash/sourceHash, and capability counts, so truc is its fetch/resolve/publish front-end — and on the admission gate: every fetched component is admitted before it joins the assembly, the differentiator vs. every other package manager. Registry/domain claims not yet made — check availability at claim time, not before. ~2-3 wk for the first add/assemble/ship loop against the local registry.

137. ✅ **`revl import cordis` stops at DSH's idiomatic TYPES — branded strings and literal-only unions (re-filed from agent/harness-m3 finding #36; follow-on to 134).** Item 134 recovered the real `PluginInventoryGateway extends TypertRemoteService` class surface (`@Remote` methods, `readonly` fields, `readonly T[]`), but the importer stops at DSH's types: (a) `Branded<'PluginEntryId'>` is an unmapped generic (known generics: Array, Promise, Record) — a branded string IS a string, so map `Branded<...>` to `Str` and resolve the alias through it. (b) `PluginFiberPhase = 'pending' | 'loading' | 'active' | 'failed' | 'unloading' | null` is refused as "a sum type with no tag" — for a literal-only union the literals ARE the tags, so synthesize a named variant (the reverse of revl's variant->TS-union lowering), and `null` -> `Opt`. Once both land, `PluginInventoryGateway.list(): PluginInventorySnapshot` imports and the harness launches the real DSH plugin. DSH checkout is the corpus; mirror the shapes in fixtures. `src/revl/import_cordis.py`.

138. ✅ **`revl import cordis` refuses/mis-parses DSH's LEADING-PIPE & multiline union style (re-filed from agent/harness-m3 finding #37; follow-on to 137).** Item 137's `Branded`->`Str` and literal-union->variant both work, but the real DSH `PluginFiberPhase` is formatted leading-pipe-per-member across lines and still fails, two ways: (a) a LEADING-PIPE union (`type X =` newline `| 'a'` newline `| 'b'`) refuses — the union parser doesn't strip the empty/leading fragment when splitting on `|` ("no revl spelling for the TypeScript type '| 'pending''"); fix: drop empty/leading fragments on the `|` split. (b) a MULTILINE union whose first member has no pipe (`type X =` newline `'a'` newline `| 'b'` newline `| null`) SILENTLY collapses to the first member (`phase: Str`, no variant, no Opt) — the type scan stops at the newline and loses continuation members; fix: the type scan must read a multiline type expression across newlines and recover all members. Both should then synthesize the variant (reuse 137's literal-union path). Repro shapes in the finding doc. `src/revl/import_cordis.py`.

139. ✅ **go's live component world is v1/v2-only — records not lowerable in a go `provide` method (harness build matrix, go tier).** The go host-Map/timer work (84/113/99) is v3, but the harness's mtier/main go components fail because records don't lower in go's COMPONENT/provide-method emitter (the roadmap already notes "go's live component world is v1/v2-only"). Bring records (and the v3 typed core) into go's component emitter so a `provide` method taking/returning a record lowers and `go test`s. `backends/go/emit.py`. Unblocks the go tier's full harness.

140. ✅ **`json_parse`/`json_stringify` have no `@rs`/`@go` bodies — the JSON wire protocol can't cross to rust/go (harness build matrix; follow-through on item 81).** Item 81 recorded this as FR-3's boundary: `Any` returns type-erase to `cordis::Value` on rust, and go's emitter can't reach `encoding/json` from a verbatim extern body — so the JSON-protocol harness runs only on py/ts. Give the stdlib JSON module real `@rs`/`@go` bodies (which likely needs the go emitter to allow imports in extern bodies, and a rust `Value`-typed shape) so structured args cross to rust/go. `stdlib/json.rvl` + `backends/{rust,go}/emit.py`. Unblocks rust's (and go's) main harness.

141. ✅ **Async emitters don't await an emission nested in a ternary/expression position (harness finding #38; recast of the earlier 'ts dispatch-await gap').** In an `async` provide method, an `emit` nested inside a ternary (or other expression position) is returned WITHOUT being awaited, leaking a coroutine/Promise. Verified on py: `async fn route(p) { return p == "go" ? emit m.complete(p) : "idle" }` -> `revl test --backend py` fails (`coroutine ... never awaited`), while a statement-level `let x = emit m.complete(p)` in the same method awaits fine. This is also the root of the web/resume_web TS dispatch gap (sync dispatch reaching async `agent.run_in`). Fix: the async await-seed must cover emissions in ternary/expression positions (await inline) on BOTH py and ts (the ts fix unblocks the web ts run) — or reject a ternary emit in an async body (awaiting is preferred; the harness's `dispatch` needs it). `backends/python/emit.py` + `backends/typescript/emit.py`. HIGH value (Cam: the highest-value picker).

142. ✅ **No lifecycle-test driver on the wasm tier (harness build matrix, wasm tier; LOW priority).** wasm runs pure components (canonical/reader/service demos) but has no driver to execute a `lifecycle test` (currently a documented skip). Build a wasm lifecycle-test driver (mirror the py/ts/go/rust lifecycle runners) so wasm lifecycle tests run instead of skip. Not blocking — documented skip today. `backends/wasm/` + `src/revl/test.py`/run driver.

143. ✅ **ts lifecycle-assert emitter's `l`/`r` temporaries collide with a user binding (TDZ) (re-filed from agent/harness-m3 finding #39).** A lifecycle test with `let r = call tools.call("read", ...)` then `assert r == "hello"` emits `{ const l = r, r = "hello"; expect(revlEq(l, r), ...) }` — the assert literal is bound to the SAME name `r` as the user binding, so `const l = r` reads the block's own not-yet-initialized `r` (ReferenceError: cannot access 'r' before initialization). py is unaffected (python scoping). Fix: collision-proof assert temp names (`_revl_l`/`_revl_r`) or rename on collision. `backends/typescript/emit.py`.

144. ✅ **Compiler/gate as a bridge SERVICE — admission/evolve cross-tier (Path A, short-term).** "admission/evolve are py-only" is really *in-process* py-only. Place the revl compiler/gate as a py-tier provider reachable over the interop bridge (item 56, TCP+mTLS), so a non-py (ts) component admits a candidate by emitting to it across the G8 boundary — no compiler rewrite. This is exactly truc's `GateKeeper` (`emit gate.admit_all` -> `compile_files`) extended from in-process to a bridge provider. Deliverable: a `Gate`/`Compiler` service (admit/compile op) implemented on py, placed as a bridge provider, with a proof that a ts consumer admits a candidate over the bridge. Cam then wires the harness's admission/evolve to it. Trade-off: the composition depends on a running py compiler service. Builds on item 56 (bridge/distribute/placement), truc's GateKeeper, `compiler.py:compile_files` ("install is admission").

145. ✅ **Self-host `lower.rvl` — a tier-portable ADMISSION gate (Path C, then).** `selfhost/` already has `lexer.rvl` + `parser.rvl` + `checker.rvl` (the frontend, cross-checked against the py originals). admission/evolve don't need the six emitters — they need the admission check (G2/G4 over the IR), i.e. lexer->parser->checker->**lower**. Add `selfhost/lower.rvl` (cross-checked against `src/revl/lower.py`) to get a revl-native, tier-portable admission gate with NO emitters — `evolve` can admit a candidate natively on any tier, deferring code generation to the py service (Path A) until Path B lands. Smaller target than the full compiler; also the first half of Path B. Builds on items 87 (selfhost lexer) + the self-host cross-check harness.

146. **Full self-host: `lower.rvl` + the six emitters in revl (Path B, finally).** The maximal story: after Path C's `lower.rvl`, self-host the six tier emitters in revl so `revl_compile` becomes a NATIVE revl operation lowered to any tier — revl compiles itself on every runtime, admission/evolve run natively with zero py, and the control plane is no longer language-bound. Huge but incremental (the checker alone is 66 KB of revl and is done). The strongest dogfood + the academic self-hosting contribution. Builds on 145; each emitter is an independently-landable, cross-checkable slice.

147. ✅ **go provide-method hardcoded receiver `s` collides with a param named `s` (latent, v1/v2/v3).** `_emit_provide_impl` (backends/go/emit.py ~1171-1177) emits `func (s *Comp) Method(...)` with `_Env(..., receiver="s")`; a provide method with a param named `s` (e.g. `fn area(s: Shape) -> Int`) emits `func (s *Registry_dir) Area(s Shape)` -> Go rejects "s redeclared in this block". Fix: rename the receiver to a reserved/underscored id (`_s`/`recv`) that `_safe_local` never mints for a user param, updating `_Env.receiver` + every ref built off it (name_ref/config_ref/req_ref/ctx_ref). Regen go goldens (v1/v2 change only in the receiver token); add a test with a provide-method param named `s`. Handed to the live item-139 agent (same `_emit_provide_impl` region).

148. ✅ **Fuller Path A: the evolve propose/regenerate LOOP over the gate service (item 144 follow-on).** Path A shipped `admit` over the bridge; evolve needs the feedback loop: admit a candidate through the `Gate` service -> on REFUSAL feed the structured why-trace (the G-rule violated, the call path, the mapped fix) to a `propose` step -> retry, bounded by a retry budget -> admitted or exhausted. The `propose` step is an EXTERN hook (the generator/agent is external) — this item builds the ORCHESTRATION + the agent-readable structured-rejection payload, not the LLM. Complements the automorph harness (which wires its evolve to this mechanism). Builds on `src/revl/gate_service.py`/`examples/gate_service.rvl` (item 144). Does NOT own the `revl` CLI verb (defer — item 123 holds `__main__.py`).

149. ✅ **Fuller Path A: network path for a non-py consumer (item 144 + item 56 follow-on).** Path A's cross-tier admit runs over a LOCAL UDS with a filtered shared IR; the clean shape is two compositions sharing only `service Gate` over item 56's TCP+mTLS NETWORK transport — but that transport is py-only today ("place network seams on py processes"), so a *ts* consumer over the network isn't wired. Wire the node/ts bridge client to speak TCP+mTLS so a ts consumer reaches a py network provider (the Gate) cross-machine, not just local UDS. Reuse item 56's TCP+mTLS + deadline/reactive-withdrawal. `src/revl/placement.py`/`backends/python/bridge.py`/`src/revl/distribute.py` + the node bridge client.

150. ✅ **`canonical.py` shared-state isolation bug — wasm canonical-ABI tests pollute across roots (surfaced by item 142; pre-existing on clean main).** Running `tests/` and `backends/wasm/` in a SINGLE pytest invocation yields 18 failures in `backends/wasm/test_canonical_abi.py` (`canonical.py:781: AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'get'`): a `tests/` test leaves shared wasm-emit/canonical state polluted so a later canonical-ABI test sees a str where a dict is expected. Confirmed via `git stash` that clean `origin/main` reproduces the identical 18 failures; both roots pass when run SEPARATELY (how CI runs them), so CI is green and this is latent. Fix the module-level/shared canonical state to be per-run (reset or instance-scoped) so a combined invocation is order-independent. `backends/wasm/canonical.py` (+ whichever `tests/` test seeds the state). LOW priority — latent, CI-green — but a real global-state leak.

151. ✅ **Fuller Path A, final step: the fully INDEPENDENT two-composition shape (follow-on to 149).** 149 wired a ts consumer to reach a py Gate over TCP+mTLS, but the example still compiles provider+consumer as ONE composition (a shared `.rvl`, so `ts_safe_ir` filtering still applies to strip the compiler extern from the node module). The clean end-state is two SEPARATE compositions sharing only `service Gate`: a consumer whose IR never contains the compiler extern at all, reaching a gate purely by address (item 55 identity + item 54 deadline) — no shared IR, no `ts_safe_ir` needed. Deliver the two-composition conductor form + a proof that an independently compiled ts consumer admits against a separately-booted py gate by address alone. Builds on 149 (transport is ready) + 148 (evolve can then run cross-composition). `src/revl/placement.py` (two-comp conductor) + a network `.toml`/example + test. Flagged in `docs/network-path.md`.

152. ✅ **Item 149 SNI bug: network seam `server_hostname` is the raw host/IP — node rejects it (found while building 151).** `placement.py::_serve_endpoint`/`_proxy_endpoint` set the TLS `server_hostname` to the raw host, so a loopback IP (`127.0.0.1`) is handed to node as the SNI servername — node rejects an IP as a legal TLS servername, so `test_full_conductor_boot_over_the_network_placement` (item 149) has only ever PASSED by skipping when cordis was absent; it fails the moment the node conductor really runs. Item 151's `[remotes]` path already dodges this with an explicit `server_hostname`; mirror that fix into 149's two helpers (carry an explicit SNI hostname, default to a cert CN/`localhost` for a loopback IP). Also confirmed alongside: `test_gate_bridge_service`, `test_mcp_leases`, `test_mcp_operator` fail only with cordis+node-v26 present (environmental/pre-existing). `src/revl/placement.py`. Small, well-scoped.

153. ✅ **Provide-key/`provides`-clause mismatch has no coded diagnostic (site-playground user hit it).** The checker rejection for a `provide` block whose key is not in the component's `provides` clause raises a bare `RevlError` at `src/revl/lower.py:3780` — generic code, no guarantee text, no fix hint (`\`skin\` is not declared in the \`provides\` clause of S`), unlike the G-/A-coded rejections. A playground user declared `provides skin1: Skin1` but wrote `provide skin { … }` and the diagnostic card couldn't name the fix. Give this rejection class a DEDICATED code (sibling of A6 'provide-methods match the service signature'; A4/A7 appear unused — pick/justify the next amendment slot per the amendment taxonomy in DESIGN.md/docs), with a hint naming BOTH fixes (rename the provide block to a declared key, or add the key to the `provides` clause), mirroring A6/G4's `GUARANTEES`+`FIXES` entries and the message-tag convention. Add `examples/rejections/` fixture + its `tests/test_frontend.py::REJECTIONS` entry (every rejection joins the executable spec). `src/revl/diagnostics.py` + `src/revl/lower.py`.

154. ✅ **Dogfood (self-host): allow a bare `var` by value in a record literal.** From item 145's dogfood log: syntax-2.0 §3.5 rejects `var n = 0 … return { x: n }` ("`var` `n` cannot be used in a record literal"), yet reading a var's FIELD (`{ x: v.field }`) IS allowed — an asymmetry. This is the dominant idiom in a token-walking parser; both `selfhost/checker.rvl` and `selfhost/lower.rvl` worked around it with value-taking `mk_*` constructors. Fix: allow a bare var in a record literal by value (the var doesn't escape, the copied value does); at minimum make the asymmetry explicit in the error. Would smooth the next self-host slice + Path B. LOCATION: enforced at `src/revl/lower.py:2381` (`_lower_pure_expr` ExprRecord/ExprVar guard), NOT typecheck.py; rejection guard at `tests/test_v2_emit.py:368` + fixture in `tests/test_frontend.py:109`. ⏸ BLOCKED: shares `lower.py`+`test_frontend.py` with item 153 — spawn after 153 lands.

155. ✅ **Dogfood (self-host): block-scope `let`, or allow shadowing across disjoint sibling blocks.** From item 145: `let` is function-scoped, so sibling `if`/`else` branches each doing `let s = …` collide ("`s` is already declared in this function"), forcing unique names (`se/sf/sv/sc`). A frequent papercut when branches do the same shape of work. Fix: block-scope lets, or allow shadowing across disjoint sibling blocks. LOCATION: enforced at `src/revl/lower.py:1793` (+ siblings 1766/1904/1947/1995) in `_lower_pure_stmt` LetStmt flat-scope, NOT typecheck.py. ⏸ BLOCKED: shares `lower.py` with item 153 — spawn after 153 lands.

156. ✅ **Dogfood (self-host): the py emitter reserves `ctx` and fails LATE at emit, not at the checker.** From item 145: a parameter/var named `ctx` fails with `EmitError: parameter name 'ctx' collides with emitter scaffolding` at `backends/python/emit.py:192` — a backend-specific, late failure for an ordinary identifier that the checker accepts. Had to rename all `ctx`→`cx`. Fix: namespace the emitter scaffolding (e.g. `__revl_ctx`), or reject reserved emitter names at the checker with a clear diagnostic instead of deep in the py backend. `backends/python/emit.py` (+ maybe a checker-side reserved-name diagnostic).

157. ✅ **Dogfood (self-host, low priority): accept `;` as an optional statement separator.** From item 145: `if (x) { a = 1; b += 1 }` → "unexpected ';'", forcing vertical expansion of every compact block — a real tax in dense parser code. Fix: accept `;` as an optional separator, or document the idiom prominently. `src/revl/parser.py`.

158. ✅ **Dogfood (self-host, low priority): allow cordis-domain nouns as contextual keywords in field/param position.** From item 145: `realm`, `intercept`, `isolate`, `in`, `with` are reserved keywords that collide with the most natural record-field/param names (`type T = { realm: Str }` → "expected ident, found 'realm'"; had to rename to `rlm`). Fix: allow these as CONTEXTUAL keywords in field/param position. `src/revl/parser.py`.
