159. ✅ **Trace schema v2: timestamps, failure codes, and emission events (unblocks 122/richer observability).** The `run.jsonl` causal trace (`why_runtime.py`, `SCHEMA_VERSION=1`) carries only load/withdraw lifecycle events — no wall-clock/monotonic timestamp, no emission event, and a FAILED withdrawal carries only a free-form `cause_trigger` detail string (no code). So `revl metrics` (122) can't compute avg lifecycle duration, failure-by-G-rule, or emission-by-capability without fabricating. Bump `SCHEMA_VERSION`→2 (reader tolerant of v1: absent field → "unavailable"): (a) add `"ts"` (float epoch/monotonic) to `make_event`, stamped in `run.py::_Driver._record`; (b) when a lifecycle settles into `FAILED`, attach the failure's diagnostic code to the cause (`cause_trigger(detail, code=...)` or a dedicated `cause_failed(code, detail)`), populated from the runtime `RevlError` via `diagnostics.classify` (READ-ONLY — do not edit diagnostics.py); (c) add an `emit` event kind `{event:"emit", component, cause..., capability:<scope>, key:<label>}`, recorded where the driver already drives `emissionsCrossed`. Keep `otel.py` (item 120) + `revl why` readers working on BOTH v1 and v2 fixtures. `src/revl/why_runtime.py`, `src/revl/run.py`, `docs/why-runtime.md` (+ `otel.py` tolerance only). Then 122 builds honestly.

160. ✅ **py emitter's OTHER reserved bare-names still fail late at emit (follow-on to 156, low priority).** 156 namespaced `ctx`/`config`/`frame` into `_revl_*`; the remaining `_RESERVED` = `{self, fmt, Pool, Map, ConfigSchema, Frame}` (the Python method receiver + `from runtime import …` names) still reject a user identifier with a LATE `EmitError` at emit, and worse, `Job`/`spawn`/`schedule` are injected as module-level runtime imports that are NOT in `_RESERVED` at all — a user name colliding with those is unguarded/inconsistent. Same class as 156. Fix: namespace or import-alias these too (or reject reserved names at the checker with a clear diagnostic). `backends/python/emit.py`. Touches import-aliasing + more golden churn, hence deferred from 156.

161. ✅ (py-tier reference; per-tier emitter routing → 167) **stdlib `Router`/`RoundRobin` load-balancer component — load balancing as spawn+realms+router, verified.** The G2 resolution for load balancing: N workers each `spawn`ed into their OWN local realm (G2 holds per-(key,realm), one `db` per realm), and a single `Router` component that `requires db in realm("w1")…realm("wN")` and `provides db` in the parent realm — so consumers see ONE provider (G2 holds) and the router distributes. Deliver a stdlib component `RoundRobin[T](n: Int, factory: T)` (and/or `LoadBalancer`) that spawns a pool (`spawn_pool(n, factory)` with `undo dispose_pool`), provides `T` by routing (round-robin / least-loaded) to the workers, and gets reactive failover FOR FREE: a dead worker's coeffect suspends its route (R2/R3, peer-death monitor), survivors continue; a replacement re-activates. Usage: `requires db via RoundRobin[Database](3, PgDatabase)`. Written IN revl, verified by the gate (its own acquire/route/dispose lifecycle is checked by G7/A8 — a load balancer that provably can't orphan workers). Builds on: spawn (all 6 tiers ✅), local realms per-spawn ✅, reactive coeffects ✅, peer-death monitor ✅, TCP+mTLS ✅ (workers can be cross-machine, cross-tier). `stdlib/` new component. Effort ~3-5 days (primitives exist; this packages them). **NON-GOAL (do NOT do): a language-level `provide db with multiplicity(3)`** — two providers of one key in one realm breaks G2 and destroys verification; the router pattern is the required shape.

162. ✅ (frontend slice — runtime routing deferred to 161) **Multi-realm `require` syntax — `requires db in realms("w1","w2","w3") strategy(round_robin)`.** Additive extension of the existing `requires <key> in realm(<name>)` binding to a realm LIST + an optional routing strategy, so a consumer (the Router of item 161, or any consumer) binds all N realms at once and routes across them. The compiler verifies EACH named realm has a provider of the key (G2 per-realm still holds — one provider per realm). Parser change (the realm-list is an extension of the single-realm concept), plus lexer/typecheck/lower for the N-way bind and the strategy annotation. Complements item 161 (the Router consumes this). Effort ~1 week, additive. `src/revl/parser.py` (+lexer/typecheck/lower). Depends on nothing new; the runtime multi-bind is the reactive coeffect model already in place.

163. ✅ **Doc/framing: the revl distribution model — load balancing = spawn+realms+router, the constraint IS the feature.** Document the design decision (so no future contributor naively breaks it): G2 (one key, one provider, one realm) and load balancing are in DIRECT tension, and the resolution is spawn+realms+router (items 161/162), NOT multiple providers. Record the two NON-GOALS explicitly: (a) NO language-level multiplicity construct (`provide … with multiplicity(N)`) — it breaks G2 and turns a verified composition language into an unverified distributed system; (b) revl ships NO built-in LB infrastructure (no Envoy/Nginx/HAProxy) — the router is a revl component you compose, the algorithm is its implementation detail, the transport is TCP+mTLS, placement decides the host. Include the distribution-model table (spawn=instances, named realms=sharding, router+pool=LB, reactive coeffects=failover, TCP+mTLS=remote, quarantine=circuit-breaking, revl_resolve=registry multi-resolution). The payoff line: a load balancer whose OWN lifecycle is compile-time verified — something no service mesh offers. `docs/` (a distribution-model design doc). Effort ~0.5-1 day.

164. ✅ **Path C slice 2 — extend `selfhost/lower.rvl` with G1 + G3 + per-realm G2 admission checks (builds on 145).** Slice 1 mirrored G2 (shared realm) + G4 + A1 into the revl-native admission gate. Slice 2 completes the structural-guarantee surface: **G1** (declared access — a component reads only what it `requires`), **G3** (acyclic dependencies — the topo sort + cycle why-trace from `lower.py::_link`), and **per-realm G2** (the `isolate`/`intercept` realm dimension, so G2 is checked per-(key,realm) not just in the shared realm). Cross-check the admission VERDICT + message text against py `compile_files`/`_link` on an extended corpus (reuse slice 1's fuzzers-as-oracles). Deferred to slice 3: the spawn/instance dimension (templates, spawn-boundary G4/G6, capability attenuation) and the remaining A1 surfaces (`_refuse_leaky_pure_arrow`, setup/activation async-reach, `await` outside a body, sync-typed-arrow leak). Now benefits from the landed ergonomics fixes (154/155/156/157/158). Continue the dogfood friction log. `selfhost/lower.rvl` + `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py`.

165. ✅ **Target-language reserved-word collision in a user identifier crashes at EMIT, per-backend, late (self-host dogfood finding from item 164; distinct from 156/160).** A revl identifier that collides with a TARGET language's reserved word — e.g. a param named `from` (a Python keyword) — type-checks and lowers fine, then the py backend aborts at emit: `EmitError: parameter name 'from' is not a usable Python identifier` (`_ident`). It slips past `compile_files` and only crashes when the emitted module runs. The revl identifier grammar is target-agnostic, but each backend has its OWN reserved set (py `from`/`class`/`lambda`; go `func`/`chan`/`range`; rust `fn`/`impl`/`match`; java …). This is DIFFERENT from 156/160 (emitter scaffolding + host builtins) — it's the target language's keywords vs an ordinary user name. Repro: `fn f(from: Str) -> Str { return from }`. Fix (preferred): uniformly MANGLE a reserved-word identifier in each backend's `_ident` (e.g. suffix `_`) so ANY valid revl name emits — target-agnostic identifier grammar; or a checker-side portability lint that fails on all backends at check time. Cross-backend: `backends/{python,typescript,rust,go,java,wasm}/emit.py` (`_ident`). Real correctness gap — a valid revl program crashes on emit.

166. ✅ **Path C slice 3 — finish the self-host admission gate (`selfhost/lower.rvl`).** The surface deferred by slices 1-2: the **spawn/instance dimension** entirely (templates, spawn-boundary G4/G6 bounds, capability attenuation, `_check_spawn_*`); the realm **prelude rule** (an `isolate`/`intercept` after an effect) + `intercept` metadata; **A1's remaining surfaces** (`_refuse_leaky_pure_arrow`, setup/activation async-reach, `await` outside a body, sync-typed-arrow leak); **G1 bare-value** name resolution (slices only resolve call/access heads); `ir_version` computation; and state-handoff / service-replacement / ambient (running-manifest) composition admission. Cross-check verdict+message against py `lower.py` on an extended corpus (reuse the fuzzers-as-oracles). Completing this makes `selfhost/lower.rvl` a full tier-portable admission gate — the last Path C step before **Path B** (item 146, self-host the emitters). May itself split into sub-slices. `selfhost/lower.rvl` + `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py`. Continue the dogfood log.

167. ✅ (py/ts/rust RUN; go/java/wasm → 173) **Per-backend emitter routing — lower a routed `require` so a Router's EMITTED body routes on every tier (follow-on to 161).** Item 161 realizes multi-realm routing in the py DRIVER (`run.py`): it resolves a routed `require` (item 162's `routes` IR) into N per-realm handles + a strategy selector + reactive failover. But the EMITTED component body can't route — each backend's `emit.py` lowers a `req` as ONE handle in ONE realm, and a routed require has no single-realm provider, so an emitted Router body sits PENDING (documented `docs/router.md §4`). To make a Router work as a normal emitted component on ts/rust/go/java/wasm (and py-emitted, not just driver-run), each backend's emitter must lower a routed require into: resolve N per-realm handles, a liveness-checking selector per the recorded `strategy`, and re-resolve-on-call for failover — mirroring `run.py`'s `_Router`. Cross-backend: `backends/{python,typescript,rust,go,java,wasm}/emit.py`. Also: `least_loaded` on a real load coeffect (currently count-based). No missing primitive — 161 proved the pattern is pure packaging. Unblocks the Router on all tiers.

168. ✅ **Self-host gate's statement reader is LINE-BASED — single-line bodies diverge from the reference + fuzzers only emit multi-line (oracle-coverage gap; from item 166).** `selfhost/lower.rvl` reads statements line-by-line (mirroring `checker.rvl`), so a component body whose statements share ONE source line diverges from py `lower.py`: e.g. `{ let store = effect Map.new() undo store.drop() isolate d in realm("r1") provide s {…} }` → reference refuses `PRELUDE`, the gate ADMITS (the `let` line swallows the trailing `isolate`/`provide`). Worse, ALL cross-check fuzzers emit multi-line only, so a single-line divergence in ANY mirrored check goes UNCAUGHT — a real oracle-coverage gap on top of the correctness bug. Fix: end a statement run at a structural boundary too (not just newline), AND add a single-line variant to each fuzzer so the oracle covers it. `selfhost/lower.rvl` (stmt reader) + `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py` (fuzzers).

169. ✅ **Path C slice 4 — arrow-color A1 + intercept metadata + spawn dimension (COMPLETES the admission gate; last step before Path B).** The remainder from slice 3's report: A1 **arrow-color** surfaces (`_refuse_leaky_arrow` provide-method sync-typed arrow, `_refuse_leaky_pure_arrow` module-fn twin, `await` outside a component body, module-fn first-class async value) — these need arrow async-color / `Async[T]` coercion modeling the gate's IR doesn't yet carry (a coherent chunk); **`intercept` metadata validation** (target must be a required key; no double-intercept — note `v2_intercept_undeclared` classifies **G1** in the reference, not yet mirrored); the full **spawn/instance dimension** (`_check_spawn_*`, templates, G4/G6 spawn bounds, capability attenuation); state-handoff / service-replacement / ambient (running-manifest) composition; `ir_version` computation. Cross-check verdict+message vs `lower.py`. When this lands, `selfhost/lower.rvl` is a full tier-portable admission gate → **Path B (146)** opens. May split into sub-slices. `selfhost/lower.rvl` + `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py`.

170. ✅ (py reference: frontend admits+colors an async timer body, py emit spawns tracked in-flight tasks + cancel inverse — no runtime change; RUNS on cordis-py, no-residue proven. Other tiers → 223) **Async timer bodies — a scheduled task can run the agent (harness milestone 60 dogfood).** Item 57's `every`/`after` timer bodies may only reach REQUIRED services synchronously (G4): `every 30s { emit sessions.append(...) }` works, but the harness's scheduled automations that RUN THE AGENT (`agent.run_in`, an `emission async` crossing) cannot be expressed in-language — the workload drives them HOST-SIDE (`_start_automations` in `tools/web_server.py`, clock-pumped alongside item 57's coeffect). Gap: a timer body that emits an ASYNC crossing (e.g. `every 60s { emit agent.run_in("cron", brief) }`). Fix: allow the timer body to carry the async-coloured arrow the way provide-method bodies do (the `Async[T]` coercion + in-flight window item 106 built for spawned handles), with the same undo/dispose contract (unload cancels, residue-free). Deliverable: `src/components/scheduler.rvl` in revl-harness grows a configurable `every <ms> { emit agent.run_in(...) }` automation (cadence + prompt + session in config), and `src/timer_tests.rvl` proves a scheduled agent run fires on `Clock.advance`. `backends/{python,typescript}/emit.py` (timer body lowering) + the runtime timer driver. Effort ~2-3 days. The host-side driver stays as the workload's persistence layer either way.

173. **Per-tier routing for go/java/wasm — blocked on RUNTIME primitives, not the emitter (from item 167).** 167 landed emitted-body routing on py/ts/rust; the other three need a runtime-layer primitive the emitter can't synthesize: **go** (stc-go 0.6.1) has no strict single-realm liveness-checked read — `Dispose()` is async so a stale value keeps resolving, and realm resolution walks the parent chain to root where the Router provides the key (G2), so a withdrawn worker's realm read falls back to the Router instead of dropping out; needs a strict, single-realm, liveness-checked read exposed by stc-go (upstream fork+PR). **java** — no real cordis4j runtime in-repo (the stub's `isolate` returns `this`, `get` has no liveness/realm resolution); needs a real cordis4j + a JRE to verify. **wasm** — the substrate has no realm registry or reactive liveness (realms are advisory on this tier). Each is a runtime gap; the emitters for these three are byte-identical today. Unblocks the Router as an emitted component on all six tiers. Coordinates with the runtime-ownership split (go/java upstream, wasm first-party).

179. ✅ (per-_Lines counter; deterministic; retires let_pattern byte-oracle exclusion) **Reference py emitter emits NONDETERMINISTIC destructure temp names (`id(node)`) — un-reproducible bytes (Path B slice-1 dogfood).** `backends/python/emit.py:1560` names the `let {a,b}=r` destructure temp `__revl_destructure_{id(node)}` — a Python object identity, not an IR property — so a re-parse of the same IR emits DIFFERENT bytes. A byte oracle can't cover `let_pattern`, and reproducible builds can't either (single-run goldens pass only because `id()` is stable within one process). Fix: derive the temp from something stable — a per-body counter threaded through `_Lines`, the node's source line/col (already present), or the sorted bound names. `backends/python/emit.py`. 180. ✅ (stdlib `Value` + total accessor fns, py bodies; per-tier @backend bodies + optional dot-method syntax deferred) **stdlib `Value`/`Json` type (or total `Any` ops) to navigate an IR document in PURE revl — the fundamental Path B enabler (slice-1 dogfood).** The checker refuses stdlib methods on an `Any` receiver (`node.args.length()` → "stdlib method `length` on a value of unknown type (G8)"), so a self-hosted emitter walking a dynamic `kind`-discriminated IR must either type the ENTIRE IR as an ADT or bridge every access through `@py` accessors (slice 1 did the latter). Every IR-consuming self-host stage (each Path B emitter) re-derives that bridge. Fix (design): a stdlib `Json`/`Value` type with total typed getters (`.str/.int/.list/.opt/.field`), OR a fixed set of TOTAL `Any` operations (get-field/length/index) erasing to host dynamic access. Makes pure-revl self-hosted backends tractable. HIGH — unblocks Path B cleanliness. `stdlib/` + `src/revl/typecheck.py`.

181. ✅ (stdlib/str.rvl gains str_newline/str_tab/str_char [pure revl via the interpolation-free backtick idiom, no builtin]; dedent now uses str_newline, the private nl() is gone. FINDING: CR U+000D cannot be authored in pure revl [parse_file reads universal-newline, folds CR->LF], so str_char excludes CR; a real CR / beyond-ASCII casing [item 189] needs a blessed per-tier fromCharCode builtin - deferred. .gitattributes pins str.rvl -text) **No literal newline / control-char in a plain revl string — text tooling needs a host bridge (slice-1 dogfood).** `"\n"` is a literal backslash-n (wanted for emitted assert messages), a plain string can't hold a real U+000A (the lexer ends a string at a newline), and triple-strings strip one leading newline — so line-joining emitter output (`lines.join("\n")`) needs an `extern fn newline() = @py { return chr(10) }`. Fix: a stdlib `Str.newline()` / char-escape helper, or a documented triple-string idiom for control chars, so every text tool doesn't reinvent a newline extern. `stdlib/` (+ docs). MED.

182. ✅ (test + lifecycle-test bodies now see module externs) **Externs are in scope inside `fn` bodies but NOT inside `test` blocks — frontend scope bug (slice-1 dogfood).** `test "t" { assert mangle("x")=="x" }` fails G1 "`mangle` is not declared in this function" while the identical call in a `fn` compiles — `_lower_tests` doesn't assemble the module-extern callable scope the way `_lower_fns` does. Forces every extern-backed helper to be wrapped in a `fn` to be unit-tested in-file (exactly how the self-host stages self-check). Fix: build `_lower_tests`' callable scope to include module externs, mirroring `_lower_fns`. `src/revl/lower.py`. MED.

183. ✅ (accepts \" / \\ in single-line strings; NOTE: selfhost/lexer.rvl scan_string needs matching escape parity before such input enters the cross-check corpus) **String literals accept `\n` but REJECT `\"` / `\\` — a single-line string can't embed a double-quote (only `"""…"""` can) (Path C slice-5a dogfood).** `"a\"b"` → `unterminated string literal` (inside a `.rvl` file it surfaces as `unexpected character '\'` while lexing) — yet `"a\nb"` compiles, so the escape table is present but OMITS `\"`/`\\`. Bit while writing string-literal programs inside `admit_src("…")` in `selfhost/lower.rvl` (made the file unlexable); the prelude/realm tests already sidestep it with `"""…"""`, a sign it's a known corner. Sharp because `\n` working invites assuming `\"` works. Fix: accept `\"`/`\\` in single-line strings for parity with `\n`, OR make the rejection NAME the unsupported escape and point at the triple-quoted form. `src/revl/lexer.py`. LOW (authoring papercut).

194. ✅ (list_contains/list_sort/list_dedup, pure-revl, sort byte-exact vs sorted()) **stdlib list/set utilities — `list_sort`/`list_contains` (or a `Set` type) (Path B dogfood, item 192).** The reference emitters build `uses`/`user_cases` as Python `set`s and use `sorted(set)` / `x in set`; revl has neither a set type nor list sort/contains, so item 192 reproduced membership with `List[Str]` + a hand-rolled `str_in` and reproduced `sorted(uses)` by walking a HARDCODED canonical order over the finite import vocabulary — which won't generalize to an arbitrary type graph. 195. ✅ (option C — new stdlib/render.rvl `render_seq` threads a counter left-to-right through pure renderers, purity intact, typed {text,counter} cell keeps a dropped counter a compile error; emit_ts commajoin adopts it, byte-identical [30 passed]. NOTE: specialised to {text,counter} not generic {value,state} because revl records unify NOMINALLY. emit_rust var_types adoption = follow-on) **Emitter-local mutable counter / gensym — the document-wide name counter is threaded through pure renderers (self-host dogfood, item 190; low).** revl has no emitter-local mutable state, so the `$revl_match_N` document-wide counter (the py reference keeps on a mutable `_Ctx`) must be threaded as `expr(node, counter) -> {text, counter}` through every combinator in exact evaluation order — ~doubling emit_ts's pure expression layer vs emit_py's nameless lambda. Fix options: a first-class emitter-local mutable counter (a `var` surviving across pure-fn calls without manual threading), a stdlib gensym/counter helper, or a documented state-threading combinator. The typed `{text,counter}` record at least makes a dropped counter a COMPILE error, not a silent wrong name. CONFIRMED across TWO emitters: ts threads a `$revl_match_N` counter, rust (item 191) threads `type Sout = {lines, ctx}` for its mutate-in-place `var_types` — both re-derive the same accumulator boilerplate. Low-priority ergonomics for self-hosting emitters; a blessed threaded-accumulator idiom would remove it. NOW THREE emitters: ts counter, rust var_types, and ts ASYNC (item 219, the hardest — an `ACx` param on ~20 fns / ~55 call sites where the reference edits ~6 lines on its mutable `_Ctx`). Strong candidate for a first-class emitter-local mutable/threaded render context. Language design.

196. ✅ (pure +42-line parser change: _primary dispatches `if` to _if_expr beside block-match, returns the SAME ExprIf node the ternary builds -> typecheck/lower/all 6 backends untouched, emit byte-identical to the ternary [pinned across py/ts/go/java/rust/wasm]; missing-else rejected; statement-if unchanged; goldens unchanged) [DECIDED: YES — add a block-bodied conditional expression so a value-producing conditional binds directly. Wave when parser/typecheck/lower free up.] **`if`-as-expression / let-bind an `if` (self-host dogfood, item 191; MED).** revl has a ternary (`c ? a : b`) but `let kw = if (c) {..} else {..}` fails to parse ("expected an expression, found 'if'") — yet the emitters must EMIT if-expressions for target languages and `_render_expr` carries a `kind=="if"` ternary, so authors rewrite each value-producing conditional as `var x = default; if (c) { x = .. }`. 201. ✅ (RESOLVED by item 228 — a `use`d module's private decls no longer leak into the importer's namespace; the `use {dedent}`+local-`rstrip` duplicate-error case is fixed) **`use { names }` selective-import doesn't scope-limit + a collision names the LOCAL, not the import (module-system DX, self-host dogfood item 199; MED).** `use "../stdlib/str.rvl" { trim, split_top }` reads as a SELECTIVE import, but all of str.rvl's top-level fns still merge into the one lowered program, so a same-named local (`rstrip`) is a hard `duplicate function 'rstrip'` — and the error points at the LOCAL def, naming neither the imported module nor that the name came from an import. Decide + fix: either make `use { a, b }` truly bring ONLY the named fns into scope, or (if import-all is intended) make the duplicate diagnostic name the imported FILE and list "names now in scope from str.rvl: …" at the `use` site. Item 199 called this "the single change that would have cut the most cost." Also minor: `Str` has `indexOf` builtin but not `lastIndexOf` (its mirror lives only in `str.rvl::last_index_of`) — a docs pointer would save a first-time porter the round-trip. `src/revl/{parser,lower,typecheck}.py` (module resolution + the duplicate diagnostic). ALSO (item 206): a `use { dedent }` silently links the module's PRIVATE helpers too, so the importer may not define ANY name str.rvl privately defines — `duplicate function 'is_word_ch'` blames the importer's own line with no hint the collision arrived through a `use`. Same fix: selective-import should not leak privates, and/or the diagnostic must name the imported module.

Note: found pre-existing go Opt emitter gaps -> 279) **`match`-block arms are `let`-only — no `var`/`while` (self-host dogfood item 198; MED).** An emitter helper naturally wanted `match ft { Some(parts) => { var ps = []; while (..) { .. }; .. }, None => .. }` but is refused at parse: "a match block arm may contain only `let` statements … may not declare `var`". This forced returning a sentinel `List[Str]` (`[]` = "not a function type") instead of the honest `Opt[List[Str]]` the value wanted — the sentinel is ergonomic, the Opt is correct. Consider allowing `var`/`while` (or a broader statement set) in match arms so imperative logic can live where the value is destructured. Surfaced writing emit_go; the same restriction shaped emit_rust/py workarounds. `src/revl/{parser,lower,typecheck}.py`. (Also reconfirms item 182 — externs not callable from `test` blocks — now hit by 4 self-host emitters.)

221. ✅ (pure revl str_utf8_bytes + utf8_encode_scalar in stdlib/str.rvl; standard 1-4 byte encoding spelled with div_floor/mod [no bitwise ops]; no surrogate handling needed since charCodeAt returns full scalars every tier; byte-exact on 1/2/3/4-byte+edges+500-case fuzz. emit_wasm non-ASCII-literal adoption = follow-on) **stdlib `str_utf8_bytes` — a code-point→UTF-8-byte primitive (from item 220; LOW).** The wasm memory ABI encodes a `Str` literal as its UTF-8 BYTES (`_wat_bytes`), but revl's str kit is code-point-based (`length()`/`charCodeAt` have no UTF-8-byte primitive) — so a self-hosted wasm emitter can only do ASCII string literals byte-exact; a non-ASCII literal (above 0x7f) needs `str_utf8_bytes(s) -> List[Int]` (the standard code-point→UTF-8 encoding: 1-4 bytes per scalar). 222. ✅ (java _method_body discriminates genuinely-EMPTY body [zero steps] -> empty no-op method, from unemittable [has steps] -> keeps the honest trap; JDK-verified boot+LIFO teardown. services_multi.rvl held out of the self-host byte-identity corpus until the fix ports to selfhost/emit_java.rvl → 265) **Reference emitters emit a THROWING stub for a legitimately-EMPTY void provide-op (self-host smell, item 216; LOW-MED).** `backends/java/emit.py`'s `_method_body` turns an empty void op (`fn reset() { }`) into `throw new UnsupportedOperationException("effectful method body")` — because an EMPTY body and an UNPORTED/not-yet-emittable effectful body are indistinguishable to the emitter. Design: docs/design/243-witnessed-externs.md] **Witnessed-inverse externs — the language form for revertible host effects (agent-tooling direction, slice 1; renumbered from the 237 collision).** Today an extern classifies `pure`/`acquire`/`emission`, so every filesystem mutation is an emission — irreversible by declaration even when a witness could make it revertible. Add the pair form: `extern effect fn rm(path: Str) undo restore(path: Str)` — an extern whose checked inverse is another extern, generalizing acquire/release pairing. The inverse must itself be host-LOCAL (a restore/rename — G5's no-emission-in-undo stays intact); the pair participates in the accumulator like a native `effect`, so unload/abort replays it, and it lands on the G8 audit surface as a *witnessed* crossing, distinct from an emission. This moves the reversible/irreversible boundary from "fixed by the world" to "moved by engineering witnesses". `src/revl/parser.py` + lower + audit; small grammar addition. 

    **Convergence note (validation of this 243-261 product-vision series).** An external reviewer, working independently, re-derived ~10 of these items from first principles: typed human approval (246/251), Secret[T] flow (256), Untrusted/Trusted input labels (249), resource budgets (260), transactional federation (118), a registry trust graph (293), proof-carrying telemetry (120+127), plus extensions of already-built handoff/swap/leases (items 53/23 + mcp/leases.py). Two independent sources converging on the same evidence-preserving-autonomy layer is strong validation that this direction is right; their sharpenings are folded into the individual items.
 

    **DESIGN (Fable-reviewed 2026-08-26; corrects the 'generalize acquire' frame).** CORE CORRECTION: witnessed is a SIBLING of `acquire`, not a generalization. They share the accumulator DATA STRUCTURE but have OPPOSITE success-path semantics: acquire's undo is a BRACKET (replays on clean unload AND abort - correct for releasing a handle, R1/R4 no-residue); a witnessed inverse is a TRANSACTION ROLLBACK (replays ONLY on abort; on COMMIT it is discharged + its witness GC'd). Lowering 'exactly like acquire' would restore every file the agent deleted on clean success - the no-residue guarantee is the WRONG contract here. So the commit/discharge split is a distinct accumulator ENTRY KIND that 243 must land from day one (even if 245 lands the commit UX). D1 = witness-as-return, AUTO-REGISTERED (no site-spelled undo, no user-visible witness threading - the accumulator owns it), and FALLIBLE: `extern witnessed[fs] fn rm(path) -> Result[FsWitness, FsError] undo restore(w)`, with Ok-conditional registration (rm on a missing path must not abort). D2 = keyword `witnessed` (not `effect` [collides with activation blocks + falsely implies bracket semantics], not `revertible` [names an aspiration TOCTOU/fallible-restore can't keep]). D3 = fourth classification, capability-scoped, but witnessed caps MUST join the same authority namespace + policy/audit accounting as emissions (with a reversibility flag) or authority is invisible to the item-33 gate. REQUIRED before implementable (Fable): (1) two accumulator entry kinds bracket-vs-transactional; (2) DECLARATION-OWNED auto-registered inverses [today the declared undo NEVER executes - every backend reads only the site-spelled step undo; and a bare witnessed call from a plain fn body registers NO inverse yet emission_analysis.py:70-71 deems it revertible - refuse witnessed externs outside effect position + fix that exemption]; (3) a NEW classification check that the declared inverse is non-emission AND non-witnessed [infinite regress] - D4 'no new rule' was FALSE, `undo some_emission(result)` passes lowering today via shared mode=undo; (4) fallible witnessed effects [Result + Ok-conditional registration] specced now, needed by 244 rm/write; (5) witness types constrained to WAL-serializable DATA [recovery.py:28-38: a closure inverse after crash is residue] + idempotent replay [abort replay can crash mid-way and recover replays again]. D5 'six backends nearly for free' is FALSE: comparable to the async-extern rollout [80/115], every tier's runtime teardown seam. Still the keystone; bigger and correctly specced now.

H1 PROVEN LIVE ON CORDIS-PY: persist-on-commit [discharged+GC'd], revert-on-abort residue-free, outside-root refused, residue ENUMERABLE via WAL. 4208 passed 0 failed additive. CAVEAT: cross-module `use "stdlib/fs.rvl"` witnessed import doesn't parse yet -> next item; tests drive the real fs bodies via the IR shape) **`stdlib/fs.rvl` — witnessed filesystem operations (agent-tooling slice 2; needs 243).** The catalog: `write` (preimage via APFS `clonefile()` CoW snapshot — O(1) even for large files; portable fallback: copy), `rm` (rename into a session garbage dir; undo = rename back), `move` (inverse move), `mkdir` (undo = remove-if-empty), directory-scale edits (git worktree/stash witness). Honest caveats to document in the module: reversal does not undo *observation* (a concurrent process that read intermediate state saw it — fine for a private workspace, weaker on shared trees); concurrent-writer races are TOCTOU-bounded, not eliminated; physical confinement belongs to the wasm tier eventually. `stdlib/fs.rvl` + py/ts extern bodies first.

Without it, 246 auto-approves witnessed fs effects silently and 245 discharges them at commit with NO enumeration, so an agent holding fs authority can witnessed-mutate anywhere the process reaches, permanently, invisible to the one commit prompt - and 246's paper claim ('allowlists cannot say which actions escaped') is hollow because revl cannot say WHERE either. Full parameterized capabilities (294) stay post-chain; this is only the workspace-root guard.

245. **Deferred irreversibility — the session commit protocol (agent-tooling slice 3; needs 243/244).** Agent actions split three ways: (a) witnessed-revertible → execute freely; (b) *deferrable* — the irreversible tail (actually emptying the garbage dir, actually sending the queued outbound emission) is postponed to a session COMMIT point; (c) immediately-irreversible — an emission whose response the task needs mid-session. On commit: one prompt enumerating exactly the irreversible residue ("empty trash: 3 files; send: 1 email") — provably exhaustive because the audit surface enumerates crossings. On abort: replay inverses via the accumulator (the `--record`/`--wal` machinery is already this shape; `revl recover` is the crash half). New: the commit boundary + the deferral queue for class (b). `src/revl/run.py` + mcp/session.py.
 

    **+ 273's commit-time witness-GC core lands HERE (Fable).** The discharge-the-inverse + GC-the-witness action on commit IS this item's commit semantics (already in docs/design/243-witnessed-externs.md); only item 273's retention-POLICY surface defers.

246. **Auto-approve-unless-irreversible — the permission policy as a type judgment (agent-tooling slice 4; needs 245).** In the MCP operator layer: class (a) auto-approved silently, (b) auto-approved with commit-time enumeration, (c) prompted per-call. The differentiating claim (defensible, and worth the workshop-paper v2 evaluation): existing harnesses approximate this with allowlists or coarse VM snapshots that cannot say which actions escaped them; here the approval decision is *derived from the checked effect type* and the residue enumeration is proof-backed. Metrics to land with it: % of tool calls auto-approved-with-proof, prompts-per-session (target ~1). `src/revl/mcp/operator.py`.

Verify: sensitive capabilities are unreachable without an approval edge; the approval is BOUND to a specific candidate hash; it EXPIRES after a duration; changing the candidate INVALIDATES it; it cannot be REPLAYED for another component. Confused-deputy-resistant human-in-the-loop.

247. 🚧 **`compensate` — typed best-effort compensation, distinct from `undo` (agent-tooling slice 5).** External effects that want best-effort reversal (DB insert → emit DELETE) are not expressible as `undo` — G5 forbids emissions in teardown, rightly (inverses must be infallible+local or recovery exactness collapses). Add a third form: a `compensate` block that MAY emit, is explicitly best-effort, runs on abort AFTER inverse replay, and lands on the audit surface (intention), never the proof surface (guarantee). The typed saga: to our knowledge no language distinguishes proof-undo from intent-compensation in the type system. Design doc first — the failure semantics (what if compensation itself fails) needs writing before code.
 

    **+ automatic compensation synthesis (external proposal #5, reconcile with this + item 243).** Classify each effect: reversible-automatically / reversible-with-a-generated-inverse / requires-explicit-inverse / irreversible. INFER an inverse only from TRUSTED metadata (e.g. `acquire filesystem.open(path)` -> `undo filesystem.close(f)`); NEVER guess an inverse for an irreversible external action (e.g. `acquire db.insert(row)` must REFUSE auto-inversion and require an explicit `undo db.delete(row.id)`); require explicit confirmation for a synthesized inverse; and MARK every generated inverse in the evidence report. Reduces agent generation burden while preserving the G4 inverse safety. 

    **+ compensation reliability CLASSES (external proposal #5, folded here).** Not every inverse is equal - distinguish exact-inverse / best-effort-compensation / forward-recovery / irreversible (`undo exact database.rollback(tx)`, `undo best_effort email.recall(msg)`, `recover forward payment.refund(charge)`), and have the audit REPORT the class per effect ('rollback verdict: database exact, email best-effort, payment forward-recovery'). Prevents the dangerous implication that 'compensation' always means true reversal. Sharpens this typed-compensate item.

Its runtime is a THIRD accumulator behavior [runs on abort AFTER inverse replay] on the Slice-2 seam, so implementation queues behind 243 Slice 2. Design-doc task running now.**

248. **Harness-boundary dogfood — run a real agent harness's tool layer through the gate (agent-tooling exit criterion; needs 243–246).** Wrap an actual agent harness's action set (fs ops, shell, one outbound API) as witnessed/deferred/emission-classified revl components and drive real sessions through admission. Measure: fraction of real agent actions that became (a) revertible, (b) deferred, (c) prompted; prompts-per-session before/after. This number — nobody else has it — is the headline evaluation for the admission-gate paper's next version, and the honest test of the "tooling language of AI agents" positioning: claim it only if (a)+(b) dominate.

249. 🚧 **Taint and provenance — prompt-injection defense derived from the
    effect system.** The agent-security failure of the era (the "lethal
    trifecta": untrusted input + private data + an outbound channel) is
    an *information-flow* problem, and revl already draws every boundary
    it needs: emissions and externs are exactly where values enter and
    leave the composition. Extend classification from *crossings* to
    *values*: what returns across a boundary is **tainted with its
    origin** (`web:example.com`, `model`, `fs:workspace`), taint
    propagates through pure computation (a fixed point over the same
    call graph the capability analysis walks), and the dangerous edge —
    a tainted value flowing into the *arguments of another emission* —
    becomes a checked event: allowed, policy-gated (33: "web-tainted
    values may not reach `send.*` without ack"), or refused. Two
    payoffs land immediately in the lighthouse workload: (a) tool results
    carry their taint into the model context, so a harness can render
    untrusted spans as untrusted to the model and the operator; (b) the
    exfiltration pattern — web content steering a send — is a *gate
    event with a why-trace*, not a hope about prompt wording. Honest
    scope from day one: taint is tracked in the runtime value
    representation on hosted tiers (dynamic, like the Int bound), the
    static half is an over-approximation (the audit already is), and
    the G8 caveat applies — a lying extern classification lies about
    taint too. Nobody has typed information flow in an agent harness;
    revl is one fixed point away from it.
 

    **+ input trust labels (external proposal #10, folded here).** Explicit `Untrusted[Str]` / `Trusted[Policy]` types; the checker REFUSES `Untrusted[Str] -> shell command`, `-> capability name`, `-> policy update` unless an explicit sanitizer or approval step intervenes. The principled prompt-injection answer: untrusted input cannot DIRECTLY create authority (stronger than an LLM 'ignore malicious text' instruction).
 

    **+ Fable: design/reconciliation FIRST - the item folds TWO mechanisms (dynamic runtime taint + static Untrusted[Str]/Trusted[Policy] labels) with no decision on which is v1, what a sanitizer/declassification step is, or how they compose. The RUNTIME taint half edits the same six-tier value-representation seam as 243 Slice 2, so it queues behind Slice 2. Static half + design-doc runnable now.**

250. **Session branching — fork a conversation *with its side effects
    rewound*.** The lighthouse workload has durable sessions; revl has the
    timeline, witnessed inverses (243/244) and the commit protocol
    (245). Composed, they make a primitive no harness has: `fork
    <session> at <step k>` — replay inverses back to step k (witnessed
    fs state included), snapshot, and branch: the user or the agent
    explores an alternative from a known-good point, with the
    workspace *actually in* the step-k state, not a transcript
    illusion of it. Deferred-class actions (245) make the semantics
    honest: branches hold their outbound sends until *their* commit,
    so exploring N branches costs zero external residue until one is
    chosen; already-crossed emissions on the abandoned branch are
    enumerated on its abort report (§6.1 — printed, never pretended
    away). Workload surface first (a sessions view offering "branch from
    here"), language surface later if a `fork` form earns it.
 

    **+ branchable / counterfactual execution history + LLM-aware replay (external proposals #1 and #4, reconcile here + item 121).** Branch a run at a step and diverge: `revl branch run --at N --name repaired`, `revl replay branch`, `revl compare`. The branch preserves component generation, source hash, provider versions, capability surface, LLM/tool calls, random seeds + clock state, WAL position, and inverse witnesses. Make the WAL LLM-aware: record each model decision (model, provider, prompt/response hashes, tool_calls, tokens, temperature, seed, latency) with replay MODES exact / tool-only / model-substitute / counterfactual. The distinctive capability: explain not just what the agent did, but what would have happened if it had acted differently. Overlaps item 121 (revl trace with LLM hops).
251. **Approval distillation — the ledger becomes policy.** Item 246
    derives the approve/prompt decision from the effect type; this
    closes the loop on the residue: the prompts that *remain* are
    recurring operator decisions ("yes, sends to api.stripe.com are
    fine in this realm") repeated forever. Distill them: the approval
    ledger (item 248's measurement substrate) detects a repeated approval
    shape and offers a **typed policy diff** — a 33-policy patch
    scoped to capability × realm × taint-origin (249), rendered with
    its blast radius ("this rule would have auto-approved 14 of the
    last 20 prompts; it can never approve an emission carrying
    web-taint") — reviewed once, applied through the audited operator
    surface (55), revocable, attributed. Prompts-per-session then
    *trends toward the irreducible floor* instead of resetting every
    session — item 248's prompts-per-session metric gains a time
    axis. The rule
    language is 33's policy, never regexes over tool names: a
    distilled rule is exactly as checkable as a hand-written one.

252. **Shell-to-witnessed lowering — the scariest tool learns the
    catalog.** The terminal tool is the workload's largest remaining
    all-emission surface: every command prompts, because `sh -c` is
    one opaque extern. But most agent shell use is fs traffic in
    disguise: parse the command line before execution and *lower* the
    recognizable subset onto the witnessed catalog (244) — `mv` →
    witnessed move, `rm` → garbage-dir rename, `mkdir`/`cp`/`touch` →
    their fs.rvl forms — executing through the witnessed path with
    real inverses; pipelines and unrecognized commands stay one
    honest emission (never guess: a command the classifier cannot
    prove fs-local is irreversible by default). The measured claim
    rides item 248's measurement: the fraction of real agent shell
    invocations that became witnessed — if it dominates, the terminal
    prompt-count collapses with it. `stdlib/shell.rvl` + the
    classifier; the classifier is pure, testable, and its
    "cannot classify" verdict is a first-class result, not a
    fallthrough.

253. **A Temporal emission target — the saga framework arrives with an
    installed base.** (Suggested by a worker agent; accepted with an
    alignment that makes it stronger than it first looks.) Temporal's
    two cardinal rules — workflow code must be deterministic, IO
    belongs only in activities — are *exactly* revl's stratum
    discipline: pure/effect code is the workflow, emissions are the
    activities, and revl checks at compile time the rule Temporal can
    only document and hope. So `revl emit --target temporal` (a target
    *variant* over the existing py/ts emitters — not an eighth
    runtime): a component's activation becomes a workflow, each
    `effect … undo …` registers its inverse in a compensation chain
    whose LIFO order is G7-derived (the code Temporal users write by
    hand and get wrong), `compensate` (247) maps onto Temporal's own
    best-effort idiom, and item 44's idempotency evidence configures
    activity retry policies *safely* — retries only where the type
    system says retry is sound. What the target buys free: durable
    execution (Temporal's history is item 47's WAL, operated by
    someone else), timers (57), signals (48), production retries — and
    a distribution channel: "keep your Temporal cluster; write the
    workflow in revl; the compensation chain is derived and the
    determinism rule is a compile error." Honest scope, stated first:
    this is *the durable-workflow subset* — realms, hot-swap and
    reactive withdrawal do not map in v1 (workflow versioning may
    carry drift-checked upgrades later, its own design note); a
    composition using them is refused for this target with a
    why-trace, never silently narrowed. Spec-first: the mapping table
    (component/effect/emission/fault ↔ workflow/compensation/activity/
    failure) is the design doc, and the exit test is one saga demo
    running on a real Temporal dev server with an injected mid-saga
    fault compensating in derived order.

254. **Witnessed network effects — the reversible boundary pushed into
    the wire, endpoint by endpoint, with evidence.** Item 243 moved
    the reversible/irreversible boundary for the filesystem by
    engineering witnesses; the same move works on APIs whose semantics
    permit it: a PUT with a GET-preimage is a witnessed write (undo =
    PUT the preimage back), a DELETE against a recreate-capable
    endpoint is a witnessed remove, a POST to a resource with a
    documented delete is `compensate`-grade, and everything else stays
    an honest emission. The evidence rule is the import family's,
    unchanged: witnessed status is *declared per endpoint* and flows
    from documented semantics (RFC 9110 idempotency via item 44,
    `x-revl-*` annotations in `import openapi`), never inferred from
    optimism — and the generated header states claim vs proof exactly
    as OpenAPI import already does. The §4.1 caveat is *stronger*
    over a network and the module must say so: reversal does not undo
    observation, and remote observers are the common case, not the
    edge. Payoff where it lands: the workload's outbound HTTP tooling
    stops being all-emission the same way its fs tooling did
    (243/244), and item 248's measurement extends to the network
    boundary — the
    fraction of an agent's API traffic that became witnessed or
    compensable, per session, with proof.

255. **Docs hygiene — workload naming consistency.** The dogfood
    workload is referred to unevenly across docs/ and dogfood/ (paths,
    milestone numbers, ad-hoc names). Sweep: refer to it uniformly as
    **the lighthouse workload**, drop absolute local paths and
    milestone numbering from public text (a findings file cites its
    finding, not the workload's internals), and keep item 248 as the
    single anchor entry. Forward rule for new text: workload framing
    only. Mechanical, one session.

256. **Capability-bound secrets — a key that structurally cannot reach
    the model.** Provider credentials today live in host config and
    enter extern bodies as ordinary strings — one interpolation away
    from a prompt, a log line, or an outbound send. Make secrets a
    runtime-injected binding, not a value: a secret is configured
    against a *capability* (`secret openai_key for model.complete`),
    the runtime injects it only inside the extern bodies of that
    capability's emissions, and **no read path exists in the language**
    — a secret is not a `Str`, cannot be interpolated, compared, or
    returned; the type has no eliminators. With taint (249) the story
    completes from both sides: secret-origin taint is refused at
    *every* sink except the bound emission, so even a host-level leak
    into the value world is caught at the boundary. The audit gains a
    secrets table: which capability holds which secret name (never the
    value), so "what could this composition leak" is an enumerable
    answer. The claim, plainly: an API key that cannot appear in the
    model context, the transcript, or any other emission — by
    construction, not by redaction. Motivating case: the lighthouse
    workload's provider keys.
 

    **+ Secret[T] information-flow (external proposal #9, folded here).** A `Secret[PaymentToken]` type the checker keeps out of specific sinks: logs, ordinary JSON serialization, an LLM prompt, an MCP tool return, an un-approved realm crossing, and any capability-boundary crossing without an explicit declaration. Complements G4 (bounds reach) with information-flow (bounds where a value may FLOW).

257. **The typed model boundary — completions with a checked shape.**
    The model call is the one emission whose response the whole system
    then *executes*, and today it returns `Str` — every tool-call
    parse is stringly, every malformed response is a bespoke bug.
    Declare the response type instead: `emission fn complete(prompt:
    Ctx) -> AgentTurn` where `AgentTurn` is an ADT (`Final(text) |
    ToolCalls(List[Call])`), the JSON schema is **derived from the
    type** (the MCP schema machinery already maps revl types to JSON
    Schema — run it at this boundary too), the provider extern passes
    the schema to constrained decoding where the API supports it and
    validates the response against it regardless, and a malformed
    response is a *typed fault* — retryable, because a completion is
    classified safe-to-retry (a read with a cost, item 44's evidence
    vocabulary), with the retry budget declared, not hoped. Downstream
    the agent loop stops parsing: it matches. The general principle is
    bigger than the model: any emission may declare a structured
    response type and get boundary validation derived from it — the
    model is just the emission where it pays most.

258. ✅ (README self-hosting badge + 8 CLI verbs + test counts corrected; distribution-model 161/162 + router §167 de-staled; ~359 em-dashes stripped across 10 files incl. site + SVG taglines; docs/DOC-STATUS.md inventories all 97 docs, 76 still needs-work + flagged) **Docs / README / landing-page refresh + de-AI-tell style pass (running; renumbered from the 243 collision with Cam's witnessed-externs item).** ~97 docs + README + site landing are stale after this session's landings (native self-hosting/compile, async timers, the value/str/list stdlib, CLI verbs metrics/profile/attest/diff, load-balancer, evolve). Refresh the front door (README incl. the stale self_hosted badge, site/index.html copy, guide-ai-agents.md, guide-humans.md, banner tagline) accurately, audit docs/*.md for staleness with an inventory, and remove em-dashes + obvious AI-writing tells throughout. Grounded in the roadmap's landed state, never aspirational; keeps the doc-example gate green. Overlaps but is broader than Cam's item 255 (workload-naming hygiene). Excludes the live-agent docs (selfhost-compile/findings, bench-selfhost).

259. **Checked parallel emissions — the latency answer, derived from
    declarations.** An agent turn's wall-clock is dominated by
    sequential tool calls, and every harness parallelizes them by
    vibes (or not at all). revl can parallelize them by *proof*: a
    batch of emissions whose declared capability sets are pairwise
    disjoint cannot observe each other's effects, and same-key batches
    are reorderable exactly when the operation declares `commutative`
    (Def. 39 — which today upgrades only *recovery* ordering; this
    gives the declaration its execution payoff). The checker derives
    the parallelizable partition of a body's emission sequence and the
    runtime fans each group out concurrently — same results, same
    audit surface, same LIFO teardown (acquisition order is recorded
    as the *sequential* order of the plan, so recovery is unchanged).
    Anything not provably independent stays sequential, silently —
    no annotation, no unsafe opt-in, no new syntax beyond what Def. 39
    already has. Exit test: a body with three disjoint-capability
    emissions completes in ~max(latencies) not sum, byte-identical
    results and audit, on py and ts; a non-commutative same-key pair
    is proven to stay ordered.

260. **Emission cardinality bounds — cost ceilings proved, not
    observed.** Runtime cost accounting says what a session *did*
    spend; the IR can say what an activation *can* spend: for
    non-looping bodies the crossing count per capability is read off
    the step list, and bounded iteration (the max_steps shape that
    landed with expressible iteration) makes looping bodies boundable
    — `caps × max-crossings` per activation, reported on the audit
    surface next to the capability table ("model.complete: ≤ 3 per
    activation; send.*: ≤ 1"). Unboundable bodies (host-driven loops,
    unbounded recursion behind an extern) report **unbounded**, loudly
    — the honest verdict is part of the feature, exactly like
    `distributability`. What it buys: an operator or a policy (33)
    can gate on declared ceilings ("no component with unbounded
    model crossings in this realm"), and a runaway-loop bug becomes a
    *compile-visible* property instead of a surprising invoice.
 

    **+ resource budgets on emissions (external proposal #8, folded here).** Beyond WHAT a component may reach, bound HOW MUCH: `emission[network.call(host=...), budget.requests=100, budget.bytes=10MB, budget.time=2s]`. Statically checked where possible, runtime-enforced, in the attestation, and ATTENUATED on delegation (a child budget is a subset of the parent's, never larger, ties to items 294/66). Extends cost ceilings from proved-maxima to enforced-budgets.

261. **Derived changelogs — the release note is computed.** Item 64
    computes the semver bump from the manifest diff; render the same
    diff as prose: `revl changelog --from a.json --to b.json` emits
    the human-readable delta — services gained/reshaped, capabilities
    added/dropped (called out first: authority changes are the lede,
    not the footnote), emissions that appeared, tiers affected — in
    the order an operator cares. The registry (49) attaches it to
    every published version; item 64's computed bump is the headline.
    Small by construction: the drift machinery already knows every
    fact; this is a renderer, and it ends hand-written release notes
    being wrong about the only thing that matters (what the new
    version may now reach).

The self-hosting capstone: component programs compile natively end to end) **Extend the fully-native compile proof to COMPONENT programs (compile.rvl / item 230 follow-up).** Item 230 wired `compile.rvl::compile_to` fully-native for the FUNCTION surface (admit_src -> lower_to_ir -> json_parse -> emit_py_src/emit_rust_src, co-compiled, no reference bytes in the chain) and proved it on the function corpus. When this lands, the capstone is complete: revl compiles component programs natively with no reference anywhere. `selfhost/compile.rvl` + tests.

    svc.op(args), Halt(why) => why }` with `op` declared `emission

Non-tail arrow still red-compiles; bare-async-arg hint unchanged. +regression fixture, goldens byte-identical) **Async arrow built in a sync frame: admitted, but the py emission is invalid Python (compiles-implies-runs violation).** Lighthouse-workload finding (the workload's agent loop; 263 is a sibling filing of the same failure class in match-arm position — numbered around it). A sync module fn that re-passes its async arrow parameter is refused with a good hint — "argument 2 ... is declared `(List[Msg]) -> Async[Str]` (async), but only an arrow may be passed into an async parameter in v1; wrap it in an arrow, e.g. `x => f(x)`". Following the hint verbatim inside the SYNC caller (`let t = g(msgs, h => complete(h), ...)` where `complete` is the caller's own async arrow param) is ADMITTED, but the py tier emits `await complete(h)` in the sync frame: `SyntaxError: 'await' outside async function` at import. Either the checker should refuse an async arrow constructed in a sync fn body (naming the async-frame requirement), or the emitter should support the pass-through by making the enclosing emitted fn async-aware. Repro is two module fns + one async service method; the workload worked around it by not delegating. `src/revl/typecheck.py` (refusal path) or `backends/python/emit.py` (both expression dispatchers) + a doc-example under docs/design/async-function-values.md.

272. **`stdlib/crypto.rvl` — classified crypto primitives (lighthouse-workload finding; the item-244 pattern).** Three independent components in the lighthouse workload hand-rolled the same crypto mechanism inside `@py`/`@ts` extern bodies within one wave: a constant-time string compare (token gate), an HMAC-SHA256 encrypt-then-MAC construction (settings store), and two HMAC webhook-signature schemes (inbound verification) — three witnesses, all security-load-bearing, none sharing code. Ship a primitive-sized stdlib kit, not a framework: `sha256(data) -> Str`, `hmac_sha256(key, data) -> Str`, `ct_equal(a, b) -> Bool` as `pure` externs with reference `@py`/`@ts` bodies (other tiers as they land), plus `random_token(n) -> Str` whose classification is part of the design (entropy draw is not `pure`; decide `acquire`-with-trivial-undo vs a dedicated reading, and document why — same rigor as 244's clonefile preimage decision). Exit: the kit admits on py+ts, a doc-example per primitive, and the workload's three hand-rolled sites replaced by kit calls with their existing tests green. `stdlib/crypto.rvl` + tests + docs/stdlib-2.0.md.

273. **Witness retention — the undo horizon, stated.** The witnessed
    world (243/244) accumulates preimages: every write keeps a
    snapshot, every rm keeps the file, and nothing yet says when a
    witness may be dropped. Unbounded, that is a storage bill; bounded
    naively, it is a silent lie — dropping a witness retroactively
    downgrades an effect from witnessed to irreversible, which is a
    *class change* and must never happen quietly. Make retention a
    declared policy with an honest surface: witnesses live to the
    commit horizon by default (commit garbage-collects the witnesses
    of committed effects — the deferral queue (245) already knows
    which those are), generation retention (65) and session branching
    (250) extend the horizon explicitly, a size budget may force early
    drops **only through the same class-change accounting**, and the
    audit answers "how far back can this composition actually revert,
    right now?" with a horizon marker ("undo horizon: commit N−3;
    witnesses dropped beyond: 2, listed"). The rule in one line: a
    witness may be dropped, but the *proof surface must say so before
    anyone relies on it* — same honesty contract as replay §4.

274. **Navigable refusals — every deny enumerates the nearest allowed
    space.** The diagnostics discipline taught agents *syntax* (each
    rejection names the fix; the friction hints landed because
    repeated refusals cost real cycles). Policy-layer denials (33,
    246, 249's taint sinks, 260's ceilings) are about to create the
    same problem one level up: an agent whose send is refused for
    web-taint will thrash — retry, rephrase, regenerate — unless the
    refusal teaches the *authority space* the way G4's message teaches
    the grammar. Every policy deny carries, bounded and computed from
    the same tables that refused: what IS allowed for this value/
    capability from here ("send.* refused for web-tainted value;
    allowed from this component: workspace-tainted sends, or
    operator-ack via commit"), the nearest policy edit that would
    permit it (the 251 distillation candidate, referenced not
    applied), and the ack path if one exists. Exit test in the bench
    idiom: a scripted agent hitting a taint deny reaches a compliant
    alternative in one step with the navigable deny vs N retries
    without it — the delta is the feature's number. The principle,
    stated once: **a refusal is a map, not a wall** — it was true for
    the checker, it must stay true for the policy layer.

276. 🚧 **[QUEUED behind 270 — touches backends/*/emit.py incl. rust, cannot run alongside 270.] Codepoint-returning scan primitive — the residual-lexer-overhead perf lever (from the item-231a finding).** Item 231a proved the inliner is NOT the current lexer's problem: item 233 already removed the per-byte fn-call layering, so the residual ~4.7x py-tier lexer overhead is the VALUE-LAYER tax — `charAt(i)` allocates a 1-char `Str` per byte, then `charCodeAt(0)`/`ord`. Add a primitive that returns the codepoint at an index directly (`str_codepoint_at(s, i) -> Int`, and/or a codepoint scan) so the lexer hot path never allocates the 1-char `Str`, and adopt it in `selfhost/lexer.rvl`. Needs a native builtin lowering per backend (`ord(s[i])` / `s.charCodeAt(i)` / rust byte-or-char index), so it MUST queue behind 270 (shared backends/rust/emit.py). Benchmark-gated: re-run tools/bench_selfhost.py, record the lexer before/after in the CPython section of docs/bench-selfhost.md. This is the perf lever the inliner could not provide. `backends/*/emit.py` + stdlib + `selfhost/lexer.rvl`.

279. ✅ (_prop_key/_member make a JS-reserved-word record field use its RAW quoted key uniformly [interface/literal/access], converging TS with PY [which never renames field keys]; bindings still mangle; string-literal index emits a raw property read not Number(...). Regression fixture runs the emitted TS under Node + cross-checks PY = same value. goldens unchanged, delta 0) **TS backend: a JSON field named by a host reserved word is unreachable on a dynamic value (`.function` access renamed, string index miscompiled) — lighthouse-workload finding.** The workload's model-provider adapter parses an OpenAI-compatible response whose tool-call entries carry a key literally named `function`. On a `json_parse` result, `tc.function.name` emits as `tc.function_.name` on the ts tier (the reserved-word sanitizer renames the ACCESS, but runtime JSON data keeps the original key — undefined at runtime), while the py tier accesses the raw key and works, so the same admitted program diverges between tiers (compiles-implies-runs violation, tier-portability form). The escape hatch also miscompiles: `tc["function"]` emits as `tc[Number("function")]` — string indexing is assumed to be a List index. This is the item-269 failure class (emitter-reserved names colliding with user identifiers, fixed there for rust record FIELDS via the A3 append-`_` mangle at decl + all uses), but the dynamic case cannot be solved by renaming: the key exists only in runtime data. Fix directions: emit quoted member access without renaming for dynamic/host-object receivers (`obj["function"]`), and/or lower a string literal index on a non-List receiver to member access, and/or a stdlib `Json.get(obj, key)`. Repro: two-line fn over `json_parse('{"function":{"name":"x"}}')`; the workload worked around it by renaming the key at its transport extern (`function` -> `func`) before the value enters the language. `backends/typescript/emit.py` (all expression dispatchers) + coverage; audit the other tiers' sanitizer lists for the same dynamic-access hole.

Opt/Result preamble gate scans rendered body. Runnable go proven [go test+build+run], goldens unchanged, delta 0) **Go backend: pre-existing `Opt` emitter gaps (found in passing by item 202).** Reproduced on plain fns (not caused by 202): bare `None` emits as an undefined identifier when passed/annotated; `Some(literal)` is typed `RevlSome[any]` so the type-switch misses; empty-list element inference produces `[]any`; a wildcard `match` on a concrete-typed scrutinee errors ("not an interface"). Fix the go emitter's Opt/None + empty-list + wildcard-match handling so these emit correct, runnable go. `backends/go/emit.py` + coverage. MED.

281. ✅ (ts json_stringify @ts body passes a replacer rendering each bigint via a quoted sentinel -> bare JSON number [digits from bigint.toString(), NOT Number() which is lossy past 2^53]; no-bigint fast path unchanged. Proven ts == py incl i64-max round-trip, vitest 126/126, through the green hook. Residual filed: ts json_parse decodes to JS number [lossy large-int] -> next item) **`stdlib/json.rvl` @ts `json_stringify` cannot serialize an `Int` field — compiles, throws at runtime (compiles-implies-runs violation; lighthouse-workload finding).** The @ts body is `return JSON.stringify(v)`, but the ts backend maps `Int` -> `bigint` on purpose (`backends/typescript/emit.py` TYPE_MAP ~line 65, for 64-bit two's-complement fidelity), and JS `JSON.stringify` throws `TypeError: Do not know how to serialize a BigInt` on any bigint. So `json_stringify` of ANY record carrying an `Int` field (token counts, ids, timestamps, an Anthropic request body's `max_tokens`) type-checks and emits clean, then dies at runtime on the ts tier only — the py tier is fine. Found in the lighthouse workload's provider layer: a component serializing a session/usage record with an Int field works on py and throws on ts; the workload dodged it by stringifying the field to Str before it entered `json_stringify`. Fix is in the @ts extern body: a replacer rendering bigint as a JSON number (e.g. `JSON.stringify(v, (_, x) => typeof x === "bigint" ? Number(x) : x)` — accept the ~2^53 practical range, or a fuller custom serializer if arbitrary precision must round-trip; hold the py tier and the `json_parse` round-trip contract as the spec). `stdlib/json.rvl` + a ts round-trip test with an Int field.

293. **Evidence-carrying registry components - registry as an evidence marketplace (external proposal #2; mostly assembly).** A registry component should carry a machine-verifiable EVIDENCE BUNDLE, not just source + manifest: attestation.json, gauntlet.json, fault-sweep.json, inverse-roundtrip.json, capabilities.json, provenance.json. Then `revl_resolve` ranks candidates by EVIDENCE QUALITY (fault-sweep coverage, valid attestation, trusted publisher), not only interface compatibility, so a resolve can prefer csv-reader 1.2.0 [fault sweep 12/12, attestation valid] over 0.9.0 [fault sweep unavailable, attestation missing]. Mostly assembly over pieces that exist (gauntlet, attest.py item 127, fault-sweep, capability surface); the new parts are the bundle format and the evidence-ranked resolve. Relates to item 49 (registry). Makes trust and discovery visible.
 

    **+ trust GRAPH + trusted-mediator marking (external proposals #14 and #11, folded here).** Make the registry a graph (verified-by / depends-on / delegates), deriving a trust score from publisher identity + attestation validity + evidence freshness + dependency trust + runtime history + reproducible-build status + capability risk + approvals, so resolution is `resolve(need, policy, trust_requirements)`, not a flat lookup. And a capability-FIREWALL component (the only holder of a raw dangerous capability, exposing only `safe_X`) can be marked a 'trusted mediator' requiring stronger evidence before replacement.

294. **Parameterized / resource-bounded capabilities - extends the EXISTING attenuation algebra (item 66) (external proposal #3, corrected).** The capability ALGEBRA the proposal asks for already exists: item 66 enforces child_caps subset-of parent_caps on spawn (attenuate / delegate / 'delegate but cannot create authority'), with docs/capability-attenuation.md + examples/tenant_attenuation.rvl + g4_spawn_widens_capability.rvl. What is genuinely NEW is making capabilities RESOURCE-BOUNDED: `filesystem.read(path="/data/incoming")`, `database.read(table="orders")`, `network.call(host="api.example.com")`, so subsumption becomes bounded (path subset-of path, table/host match) and a spawned worker gets a truly attenuated subset (`spawn Worker with capabilities[filesystem.read("/tmp/job-42"), database.read("orders")]`). LARGE: the capability type gains parameters, and G4 + attenuation (66) + all six backends + the wasm least-authority policy (289) must handle bounded subsumption. Design-first. Unlocks real least-authority for agent security, multi-tenant, embedded, wasm hosts, IAM policy generation, compliance evidence.
 

    **+ lease-bound (expiring) capabilities (external proposal #6, folded here).** A capability that auto-revokes: `filesystem.write(path="/tmp/job-42", until=job_finished)`, bound to a component generation, a task, a realm, a human approval, a time duration, or a max call count; the runtime revokes when the lease ends. Temporary authority is safer than permanent for agents. Builds on component leases (mcp/leases.py, docs/component-leases.md) + this item's parameterized capabilities.

295. 🚧 **Deterministic concurrency / schedule testing - `revl test --schedule-seed` (external proposal #7; the standout net-new).** The fault sweep tests failure POINTS; concurrent systems also fail because operations INTERLEAVE differently. A deterministic scheduler explores interleavings (A1->B1->A2->B2, A1->A2->B1->B2, B1->A1->B2->A2, ...) and for each schedule checks: no residue, no deadlock, no capability violation, stable final state, correct teardown order, no use-after-withdrawal. A schedule replays from a seed (`revl test --schedule-seed 48192`). DISTINCT from item 259 (which parallelizes emissions for LATENCY, not tests interleavings for CORRECTNESS); this extends revl's execution-based verification to concurrency. `src/revl/test.py` + a deterministic scheduler + the fault machinery. HIGH novelty.
 

    **+ Fable: design + py-tier prototype, COORDINATED with 243 Slice 2a - it instruments the py runtime loop Slice 2a is editing this week. Decide the interleaving alphabet [steps / emissions / async-extern completions], which tier the scheduler instruments, and seed->schedule mapping. Design-doc runnable now; the py prototype lands after Slice 2a. Route any shared-witnessed-resource interleaving findings into the 308 design pass.**

296. **Component contract negotiation - safe adapter synthesis (external proposal #1).** When a consumer's required interface and a candidate's provided interface differ but are safely bridgeable (consumer wants `cache.get(Key) -> Opt[Value]`, candidate provides `cache.get(Key, Options) -> Result[Value, Error]`), generate an ADAPTER, but ONLY when: the type conversion is explicit (default `Options={}`, `Result.Err -> Opt.None`), NO capability expands, lifecycle guarantees remain valid, error semantics are not silently weakened, and the adapter is included in the diff + attestation. Turns registry resolution from accept/refuse into 'compatible-with-adapter' with the adapter itself verified. Relates to item 49 (registry) + 123 (diff).
 

    **+ verified adapter marketplace (external proposal #15, folded here).** With truc + the registry + WIT import/export + composition diff, support a marketplace of verified adapters (PostgresV1->Database, SQLiteV2->Database, MCPToolX->SearchService, CordisPluginY->FileService), each carrying its interface transform, capability transform, lifecycle mapping, evidence, compatibility proof, and version bounds. Scales this safe-adapter item to heterogeneous runtimes + external plugins. Relates to 293 (evidence registry).

307. **Minimal-capability repair patch - extend `revl profile` (external proposal #12; near-term).** `revl profile` (item 124, done) already detects capability over-declaration; add a least-authority PATCH: declared vs observed-reachable -> a suggested `emission[...]` (e.g. declared [filesystem.read, filesystem.write, network, shell], observed [filesystem.read] -> patch `emission[filesystem.read]`). 308. **[PRODUCT-VISION - design pass with 243-261] Effect ownership modes - owned / borrowed / shared (external proposal #3).** Explicit ownership on resources; verify: the OWNER performs the final inverse; a BORROWER cannot close an owned resource; a borrowed handle cannot outlive its owner; SHARED handles use reference-counted or lease-based teardown; a realm boundary requires explicit transfer. Extends the G4/G7 resource model from 'there is an inverse' to 'the correct component owns the inverse'. Deep type-system change.

309. **[PRODUCT-VISION - design pass with 243-261] Idempotent inverse verification (external proposal #4).** An `idempotent` effect declaration; require/test `undo(undo(state)) = undo(state)`; for external systems require an idempotency key (`inventory.release(reservation, idempotency_key=reservation.id)`). Moves G7 from local-LIFO correctness to reliable DISTRIBUTED rollback (crash recovery / network retry / duplicate delivery / operator replay / partial failure). Relates to 243/247 and to distributed correlation (folded on 118).

310. **[PRODUCT-VISION - design pass with 243-261] Capability-aware caching (external proposal #9).** The language distinguishes `cache pure_fn` / `cache capability_result` / `cache external_effect`, and REQUIRES invalidation/freshness semantics for cached external results (`cache user_profile invalidated_by user.updated ttl 5m`). A cache changes authority + consistency, not just performance. Useful for agent memory, service discovery, registry resolution, multi-realm state.

311. ✅ (ts @ts json_parse replaced with a number-preserving recursive-descent parse: a JSON integer literal [no dot/exponent] decodes to a JS bigint = revl Int at full i64 precision; a float [dot/e/E] to a JS number = revl Float. A JSON.parse reviver can't recover the value [handed the already-rounded number], hence the hand parse. Round-trips with 281's stringify replacer; ts == py both directions. 37 json/ts tests pass, goldens unchanged) **ts `json_parse` loses precision on a large integer (found by item 281).** The ts `@ts json_parse` uses builtin `JSON.parse`, which decodes every JSON number to a JS `number` (f64), so an integer beyond 2^53 recovered THROUGH parse is lossy on ts while py keeps it exact - a silent cross-tier value divergence on the parse direction (281 fixed the stringify direction). Fix: a bigint-aware ts json_parse (reviver / a number-preserving parse) that decodes an integer literal to a JS `bigint` (revl `Int`), matching py. `stdlib/json.rvl` @ts + backends/typescript. MED. Cross-check ts == py on a large-int round-trip.

312. ✅ (lower.py threads each node's own ComponentDecl.source/FnDecl.source into diagnostics instead of the merged program.filename [=argv[0]], so a span in a later file prints THAT file's name + line; single-file/in-memory byte-identical [nodes without .source fall back]. Covers audit/compile/all verbs + --json-diagnostics. 3 regression tests, suite 0 failed) **Multi-file `revl audit`/`compile` error messages pin the location FILENAME to the FIRST source argument, not the file that actually holds the offending line - lighthouse-workload finding.** When several sources are passed together (`revl audit a.rvl b.rvl c.rvl`), a diagnostic whose span lives in a LATER file is printed with the first file's name and the real file's line number, so the two halves of the location disagree. Repro from the workload: `revl audit src/services.rvl src/components/session_ledger.rvl src/components/durable_sessions.rvl` prints `error: src/services.rvl:103: ... not a declared requirement of DurableSessions` for a line that lives in `durable_sessions.rvl:103` - and `services.rvl` is only 81 lines long, so the location is impossible on its face. Reordering the argument list moves the reported filename to whatever is argv[0] while the line number keeps tracking the true source file, which localizes the bug: the filename is taken from the first source, the line from the correct one. This actively misled a workload contributor, who went looking in `services.rvl` for a `drop_last` helper that is defined only in the component files. Fix: carry each parsed source's own path through to the diagnostic's location record so a multi-file audit/compile names the file that actually contains the span. `src/revl/` (the multi-source location/diagnostic plumbing). LOW (cosmetic, but costs real debugging time on any multi-file invocation). Minimal repro: two files that each declare a top-level `fn` of the same name, audited together; compare the reported path to where the name truly lives.

313. **Go backend: a match on an Opt/Result CONSTRUCTOR-LITERAL scrutinee emits invalid Go (fuzzer-found, distinct from 280/302/304).** `match Ok(1) { Ok(o) => o, Err(e) => 0 }` (a literal scrutinee, not a variable) lowers to `switch _m := RevlOk[..]{..}.(type) {` - an UNPARENTHESIZED composite literal in the type-switch init clause, so Go reads the `{` as the switch body (`expected '}', found Value`); also `.(type)` needs an interface not a concrete struct. `match r {..}` on a variable works, `match None {..}` works; only Ok/Err/Some literal scrutinees break. Fix in `backends/go/emit.py` v3 match lowering (~L3408): bind the scrutinee to an interface-typed temp before the type switch. Fixture: examples/regressions/fuzz_go_matchlit_typeswitch.rvl. compiles-implies-runs violation.

314. **Go tier: `go vet` (run by `go test`) rejects tautological boolean ops that revl/py admit - DECISION (fuzzer-found).** `return (false || false)` runs on py (false) but the go tier's `go test ./...` invokes `go vet`, whose `bools` analyzer rejects `a||a`/`a&&a` (`redundant or`). Confirmed: false||false, true||true, x||x, x&&x fail; true||false passes. Not a codegen bug, a test-harness strictness gap. DECISION: either run the go tier with `-vet=off` (`src/revl/test.py` go runner ~L374 - accept that revl admits redundant bools and go-vet is stricter) OR fold identical-operand boolean ops in `backends/go/emit.py` (make revl's semantics match go-vet). Fixture: examples/regressions/fuzz_go_vet_redundant_bool.rvl.

318. 🚧 **Witnessed effects usable in a PROVIDE-METHOD (on-demand, per-tool-call) - THE dominant H1 gate (harness dev).** 243/244/2b only allow a witnessed effect in the component ACTIVATION body (runs once at load). But an agent's fs mutation fires from a provide-METHOD (ToolRegistry.call -> a provide-method body, per tool call), which revl REFUSES today ('only spawn may be acquired inside a provide-method body'), closed on every tier. So even with all of 243/244/2b landed, the REAL agent use case is dead. Open the witnessed position in a provide-method: a method-body witnessed effect registers its transactional inverse into the enclosing component's activation accumulator (which Slice 2a/2b already discharge on commit / revert on abort), so a per-tool-call fs mutation persists on clean session-end and reverts on abort. If per-call effects genuinely need a SESSION-scoped accumulator beyond the activation frame (deferred to 245's commit), report that as the design fork. Prove H1 from a provide-method on py first, then the tiers 2b delivers. `src/revl/lower.py`/typecheck + the per-tier method->Frame wiring.

322. **Extend WAL / crash-recovery to the non-py tiers (flagged by Slice 2b go + java).** The witnessed teardown loop landed on all six tiers (persist-on-commit / revert-on-abort works IN-PROCESS everywhere), but the WAL discharge-descriptor + `revl recover` crash-recovery is PY-ONLY: `--record`/`--wal` only wires through the in-process py driver (`backends/python/replay.py` + `src/revl/recovery.py`), while rust/go/java/wasm produce residue in-memory (e.g. go's `RevlTeardownRecord`, java's abandonment records) with no durable sink, and `run_java.py`/etc spawn a subprocess with no host recording channel. So a PROCESS CRASH mid-session is only recoverable on py. Extend the WAL emission + a host recording channel to the non-py tiers so crash-recovery is six-tier (the teardown contract already names WAL as a py-only owned deliverable). NOT H1-blocking (in-process abort reverts on every tier); needed for the full distributed/crash-safe story. `src/revl/recovery.py`/`replay.py` + per-tier run harnesses. Relates to items 15 (snapshot) + 118 (remote).
